-- Classroom access is no longer a per-student permission managed from the
-- People page. Every student needs to reach /classroom to see the bootcamps
-- assigned to them; bootcamp visibility itself is controlled by classroom_items
-- assignments and enforced by BootcampGate, not by a global flag.
--
-- The column default is already true (see 20260416015637), so new students are
-- covered. This backfills any existing student whose classroom access was
-- revoked under the old broad-permission model.
UPDATE public.profiles
SET has_classroom_access = true
WHERE has_classroom_access = false;

import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useLocation, useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { authUrlWithReturnTo } from "@/lib/authRedirect";

export interface UserPermissions {
  has_bootcamp_access: boolean;
  has_classroom_access: boolean;
  has_analytics_access: boolean;
  has_schedule_access: boolean;
  has_practice_access: boolean;
  has_drill_access: boolean;
  has_waj_access: boolean;
  has_journal_access: boolean;
  has_flagged_access: boolean;
  has_chat_access: boolean;
  has_export_access: boolean;
  has_ta_access: boolean;
  has_activity_access: boolean;
  is_admin: boolean;
  loading: boolean;
}

const ACCESS_FLAGS = [
  "has_bootcamp_access",
  "has_classroom_access",
  "has_analytics_access",
  "has_schedule_access",
  "has_practice_access",
  "has_drill_access",
  "has_waj_access",
  "has_journal_access",
  "has_flagged_access",
  "has_chat_access",
  "has_export_access",
  "has_ta_access",
  "has_activity_access",
] as const;

export type PermissionFlag = (typeof ACCESS_FLAGS)[number];

// Narrow session-death classifier. Only three conditions count:
//  1. supabase.auth.getSession() returns no session while context says user is set
//  2. Supabase query error with HTTP status 401
//  3. Error message mentioning JWT / token-expired / invalid_token explicitly
// Everything else (including RLS 403s and null profile rows) is legitimate
// permission-denied and must fall through to the normal LockedModule path.
function isSessionDeadError(err: any): boolean {
  if (!err) return false;
  if (err.status === 401) return true;
  const msg = String(err.message || "").toLowerCase();
  return (
    msg.includes("jwt") ||
    msg.includes("token is expired") ||
    msg.includes("token expired") ||
    msg.includes("invalid_token")
  );
}

type PermissionFlags = Omit<UserPermissions, "loading">;

const DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS: PermissionFlags = {
  has_bootcamp_access: false,
  has_classroom_access: false,
  has_analytics_access: false,
  has_schedule_access: false,
  has_practice_access: false,
  has_drill_access: false,
  has_waj_access: false,
  has_journal_access: false,
  has_flagged_access: false,
  has_chat_access: false,
  has_export_access: false,
  has_ta_access: false,
  has_activity_access: false,
  is_admin: false,
};

// Sentinel thrown by the query fn when the session is dead. The hook reacts to
// it once (sign out + /auth bounce) instead of navigating from inside the
// queryFn, which may run detached from a render.
class SessionExpiredError extends Error {}

export function useUserPermissions(): UserPermissions {
  const { user, authReady, signOut } = useAuth();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const location = useLocation();

  const query = useQuery<PermissionFlags>({
    // Cached per user, shared across all ProtectedRoute / consumer mounts, so
    // navigating between gated pages reads cached permissions synchronously
    // instead of re-fetching (and re-showing the full-screen loading gate).
    queryKey: ["user-permissions", user?.id],
    enabled: authReady && !!user,
    staleTime: 5 * 60_000,
    gcTime: 30 * 60_000,
    // Retry transient failures a couple times, but never retry a dead session
    // (that path signs the user out).
    retry: (count, err) => !(err instanceof SessionExpiredError) && count < 2,
    queryFn: async () => {
      const userId = user!.id;

      // Pre-check: is the session still alive on the client?
      const { data: { session } } = await supabase.auth.getSession();
      if (!session) throw new SessionExpiredError();

      // Fetch access flags from profile
      const { data: profile, error: profileErr } = await supabase
        .from("profiles")
        .select(ACCESS_FLAGS.join(", "))
        .eq("class_id", userId)
        .maybeSingle();
      if (isSessionDeadError(profileErr)) throw new SessionExpiredError();
      // A non-session read failure (transient network / 5xx) must NOT be
      // silently converted to all-false permissions; throw so TanStack Query
      // records the error and retries instead of caching bogus denied flags.
      if (profileErr) throw profileErr;

      // Fetch admin role
      const { data: roleData, error: roleErr } = await supabase
        .from("user_roles")
        .select("role")
        .eq("user_id", userId)
        .eq("role", "admin")
        .maybeSingle();
      if (isSessionDeadError(roleErr)) throw new SessionExpiredError();
      if (roleErr) throw roleErr;

      const profileRow = (profile ?? null) as unknown as Record<string, boolean> | null;
      const flagValues = { ...DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS };
      for (const flag of ACCESS_FLAGS) {
        flagValues[flag] = profileRow?.[flag] ?? false;
      }
      flagValues.is_admin = !!roleData;

      // Touch last_seen_at (fire-and-forget). Non-blocking, but check the
      // returned error and warn rather than discarding it entirely.
      supabase
        .from("profiles")
        .update({ last_seen_at: new Date().toISOString() })
        .eq("class_id", userId)
        .then(({ error }) => {
          if (error) console.warn("[permissions] last_seen_at touch failed:", error.message);
        });

      return flagValues;
    },
  });

  // A dead session bounces to /auth once. Preserve lsatu_saved_email so
  // re-login is smooth after an expiry bounce, and carry where they were: a
  // bare "/auth" discarded the pathname, query, and hash, so a student whose
  // token expired on an assignment link lost the link as well as the session.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (query.error instanceof SessionExpiredError) {
      const returnTo = `${location.pathname}${location.search}${location.hash}`;
      (async () => {
        await signOut({ keepSavedEmail: true });
        navigate(authUrlWithReturnTo(returnTo), {
          replace: true,
          state: { reason: "session_expired" },
        });
      })();
    } else if (query.error) {
      console.error("Failed to fetch permissions", query.error);
    }
    // `location` is intentionally not a dependency: this must capture where the
    // user was when the session died, not follow them as they navigate.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, [query.error, signOut, navigate]);

  return {
    ...DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS,
    ...(query.data ?? {}),
    // Only the true cold load (no cached data) blocks; cached reads and
    // background refetches keep loading false, so gated navigations don't flash.
    loading: authReady && !!user && query.isPending,
  };
}

export const PRIMARY_ADMIN_EMAIL = 'contact@aspiringattorneys.com';

export const isPrimaryAdmin = (email: string | null | undefined) =>
  (email || '').toLowerCase() === PRIMARY_ADMIN_EMAIL;

export interface ManagedUser {
  class_id: string;
  email: string;
  display_name: string | null;
  role: string;
  last_seen_at: string | null;
  has_bootcamp_access: boolean;
  has_classroom_access: boolean;
  has_analytics_access: boolean;
  has_schedule_access: boolean;
  has_practice_access: boolean;
  has_drill_access: boolean;
  has_waj_access: boolean;
  has_flagged_access: boolean;
  has_chat_access: boolean;
  has_export_access: boolean;
  has_ta_access: boolean;
  has_journal_access: boolean;
  has_activity_access: boolean;
}

export interface AdminAnalytics {
  total_users: number;
  active_today: number;
  active_week: number;
  admin_count: number;
  feature_adoption: Record<string, number>;
  total_attempts: number;
  week_attempts: number;
}

// Every door a student can be given, grouped the way the classroom rail groups
// them, and labelled in the words the rail uses. This list is the admin UI: a
// flag that is missing here has no toggle anywhere, which is how
// `has_journal_access` and `has_activity_access` came to be ungrantable while
// the rail happily padlocked Wrong answers and Activities against them.
//
// Keep it in step with `ALL_FLAGS` in supabase/functions/admin-manage-users.
// The server writes what it is told; if the client cannot name a flag, no
// amount of pressing "grant all" will turn it on where Joshua can see it.
export const FLAG_GROUPS = [
  {
    label: 'Practice',
    flags: [
      { key: 'has_drill_access', label: 'Drill' },
      { key: 'has_practice_access', label: 'Practice tests' },
      { key: 'has_activity_access', label: 'Activities' },
      { key: 'has_bootcamp_access', label: 'Bootcamps' },
    ],
  },
  {
    label: 'Review',
    flags: [
      { key: 'has_journal_access', label: 'Wrong answers' },
      { key: 'has_waj_access', label: 'Wrong answer journal' },
      { key: 'has_flagged_access', label: 'Flagged' },
      { key: 'has_analytics_access', label: 'Analytics and History' },
      { key: 'has_export_access', label: 'Export' },
    ],
  },
  {
    label: 'Classroom',
    flags: [
      { key: 'has_classroom_access', label: 'Classroom' },
      { key: 'has_chat_access', label: 'Messages' },
      { key: 'has_schedule_access', label: 'Schedule' },
      { key: 'has_ta_access', label: 'TA assignments' },
    ],
  },
] as const;

export const CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS = FLAG_GROUPS.flatMap((group) => group.flags.map((flag) => flag.key));
/**
 * What a paying student gets regardless of which template is applied. Templates
 * force these back on after the explicit list, so "Trial" can narrow practice
 * without taking away the room the student stands in.
 */
export const DEFAULT_ON_FLAG_KEYS = [
  'has_practice_access',
  'has_bootcamp_access',
  'has_classroom_access',
  'has_analytics_access',
  'has_schedule_access',
  'has_waj_access',
  'has_journal_access',
  'has_flagged_access',
  'has_chat_access',
  'has_export_access',
  'has_ta_access',
  'has_activity_access',
] as const;
/** Deduped: every configurable flag is now also a default-on candidate. */
export const ALL_FLAG_KEYS = [...new Set<string>([...CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS, ...DEFAULT_ON_FLAG_KEYS])];

export const ALL_FLAG_LABELS: Record<string, string> = {};
FLAG_GROUPS.forEach((group) => group.flags.forEach((flag) => {
  ALL_FLAG_LABELS[flag.key] = flag.label;
}));

export function accessTemplate(enabled: string[]): Record<string, boolean> {
  const out: Record<string, boolean> = {};
  for (const key of CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS) out[key] = enabled.includes(key);
  for (const key of DEFAULT_ON_FLAG_KEYS) out[key] = true;
  return out;
}

export const ROLE_TEMPLATES: { id: string; label: string; description: string; flags: Record<string, boolean> }[] = [
  {
    id: 'trial',
    label: 'Trial',
    description: 'Practice and Drill only.',
    flags: accessTemplate(['has_practice_access', 'has_drill_access']),
  },
  {
    id: 'standard',
    label: 'Standard',
    description: 'Core tools plus classroom.',
    flags: accessTemplate([
      'has_practice_access',
      'has_drill_access',
      'has_classroom_access',
      'has_bootcamp_access',
    ]),
  },
  {
    id: 'full',
    label: 'Full',
    description: 'All configurable access.',
    flags: accessTemplate(CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS),
  },
];

export function templateMatches(user: Record<string, unknown>, template: Record<string, boolean>): boolean {
  return CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS.every((key) => Boolean(user[key]) === template[key]);
}

  const toggleAccess = async (classId: string, field: string, value: boolean) => {
    setUsers((prev) => prev.map((user) => (user.class_id === classId ? { ...user, [field]: value } : user)));
    try {
      const res = await supabase.functions.invoke('admin-manage-users', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: { class_id: classId, field, value },
      });
      if (res.error) throw res.error;
      // Trust the row the server wrote back rather than the optimistic guess,
      // so a toggle that never landed can't look like it worked.
      const written = (res.data as { profile?: Record<string, unknown> } | null)?.profile;
      if (!written || written[field] !== value) throw new Error('Permission was not saved');
    } catch (error) {
      setUsers((prev) => prev.map((user) => (user.class_id === classId ? { ...user, [field]: !value } : user)));
      console.error('toggleAccess failed', { classId, field, value, error });
      toast.error('Failed to update permission');
    }
  };

  // name, the intake email re-armed, every paid door shut. Used to test the

import { createClient } from "npm:@supabase/supabase-js@2";

const corsHeaders = {
  "Access-Control-Allow-Origin": "*",
  "Access-Control-Allow-Headers": "authorization, x-client-info, apikey, content-type",
};

// The primary admin account can never be demoted, so the system always retains
// at least one admin and can never be locked out of role management.
const PRIMARY_ADMIN_EMAIL = "contact@aspiringattorneys.com";

const ALL_FLAGS = [
  "has_bootcamp_access",
  "has_classroom_access",
  "has_analytics_access",
  "has_schedule_access",
  "has_practice_access",
  "has_drill_access",
  "has_waj_access",
  "has_flagged_access",
  "has_chat_access",
  "has_export_access",
  "has_ta_access",
  "has_journal_access",
  "has_activity_access",
];

async function verifyAdmin(req: Request) {
  const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")!;
  const serviceRoleKey = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")!;
  const anonKey = Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_ANON_KEY")!;

  const authHeader = req.headers.get("Authorization");
  if (!authHeader?.startsWith("Bearer ")) throw new Error("Missing auth");
  const token = authHeader.slice("Bearer ".length);

  const anonClient = createClient(supabaseUrl, anonKey);
  const { data: claimsData, error } = await anonClient.auth.getClaims(token);
  if (error || !claimsData?.claims?.sub) throw new Error("Unauthorized");
  const user = { id: claimsData.claims.sub as string };

  const admin = createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceRoleKey);
  const { data: roleData, error: roleLookupError } = await admin
    .from("user_roles")
    .select("role")
    .eq("user_id", user.id)
    .eq("role", "admin")
    .maybeSingle();

  if (roleLookupError) {
    console.error("verifyAdmin: role lookup failed", {
      user_id: user.id,
      error: roleLookupError,
    });
    throw new Error("Forbidden");
  }
  if (!roleData) throw new Error("Forbidden");

  return { admin, callerId: user.id };
}

function jsonRes(data: unknown, status = 200) {
  return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), {
    status,
    headers: { ...corsHeaders, "Content-Type": "application/json" },
  });
}

      const url = new URL(req.url);
      const mode = url.searchParams.get("mode");

          </Button>

  const toggleAccess = async (classId: string, field: string, value: boolean) => {
    setUsers((prev) => prev.map((user) => (user.class_id === classId ? { ...user, [field]: value } : user)));
    try {
      const res = await supabase.functions.invoke('admin-manage-users', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: { class_id: classId, field, value },
      });
      if (res.error) throw res.error;
      // Trust the row the server wrote back rather than the optimistic guess,
      // so a toggle that never landed can't look like it worked.
      const written = (res.data as { profile?: Record<string, unknown> } | null)?.profile;
      if (!written || written[field] !== value) throw new Error('Permission was not saved');
    } catch (error) {
      setUsers((prev) => prev.map((user) => (user.class_id === classId ? { ...user, [field]: !value } : user)));
      console.error('toggleAccess failed', { classId, field, value, error });
      toast.error('Failed to update permission');
    }
  };

  // One button, whole account: revoking shuts every door except the classroom
  // home page, so the student can still sign in and land somewhere truthful.
  // Granting opens everything back up.

## assistant

Here is how it works today. Everything below is verified against the code on `origin/main` (which matches what I saw live at lsatprep.study/teacher/students in the in-app browser) unless marked otherwise.

## The data model

Access is 13 boolean columns on `profiles`, one per "door":

`has_drill_access`, `has_practice_access`, `has_activity_access`, `has_bootcamp_access`, `has_journal_access`, `has_waj_access`, `has_flagged_access`, `has_analytics_access`, `has_export_access`, `has_classroom_access`, `has_chat_access`, `has_schedule_access`, `has_ta_access`

- **Every column defaults to `true`.** A new signup gets everything the moment the profile row is created. There is no "pending approval" state.
- `has_classroom_access` is effectively dead as a control: a 2026-06-30 migration force-set it true for everyone, and the UI never turns it off (the student needs somewhere to land).
- Admins (`user_roles.role = 'admin'`) bypass every flag.

## How it's enforced

[useUserPermissions.ts](src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts) reads the 13 flags once per login (cached 5 minutes). [ProtectedRoute](src/components/ProtectedRoute.tsx) checks the route's flag; if false, the student sees [LockedModule](src/components/LockedModule.tsx) ("Not open yet... send Joshua a message"). The classroom rail also padlocks items against the same flags.

Consequence: **a revoke takes up to 5 minutes to land** for a student already signed in, because the flags are cached client-side. There's no server push.

## What you can actually do from the UI (live)

On `/teacher/students`, each student row has exactly one button:

- **Revoke All Access** (red): confirm dialog, then sets all 13 flags false except `has_classroom_access`. Student keeps login and home page; every door locks.
- **Grant Full Access** (green): sets all 13 true.
- The button state is derived: it shows "Revoke" if *any* flag other than classroom is on. So a partially locked student still reads as "has access".
- The row text shows only `Drill on/off`, so the summary tells you about one flag, not the account state.

Expanding a row gives Promote/Demote admin and **Reset onboarding** (wipes intake, name, and locks all doors except classroom/messages/schedule).

Both go through the `admin-manage-users` edge function (`apply_template` action), which writes the columns with the service role and returns the written row.

## What exists in code but is not reachable

- **Per-flag toggles and templates (Trial / Standard / Full)** live in [AdminDashboard.tsx](src/pages/AdminDashboard.tsx) and [adminUsers.ts](src/lib/adminUsers.ts), but `AdminDashboard` is not mounted on any route. The templates are defined and the server supports them; you just have no way to click them.
- The edge function also accepts a single-flag `toggle` action and a `bulk_user` action; the live UI only calls `apply_template`.
- My local checkout (`feat/calendar-strip-deeplink`) has an older version of this button labelled "Grant/Revoke Drill Access" that only flips `has_drill_access`. That is not what's deployed.

## Where it falls short as an on/off switch

1. **No neutral default.** New signups are fully on. If you want "off until I say so", that's a column-default change plus a signup-time decision, not a UI change.
2. **Binary and lossy.** Revoke flattens whatever custom mix a student had; Grant restores everything, not what they had before. No record of the prior state.
3. **No expiry or reason.** Nothing stores *why* or *until when*. A paused student and a former student look identical.
4. **No audit trail.** `profiles` just holds the current booleans; nothing logs who flipped what and when.
5. **Slow to take effect** (5-minute cache) and no way to force a sign-out.
6. **Row summary lies by omission** ("Drill on" while nine other doors are off).

If the goal is a clean "is this person a current student" switch, my recommendation is a single `access_status` (`active | paused | off`) with `paused_at`/`reason`, computed into the 13 flags server-side, plus an audit table and cache invalidation on change. The 13 columns stay for fine-grain, but the switch you reach for daily becomes one field with one meaning. Say the word and I'll write that up as a spec before touching anything.

import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { toast } from "sonner";
import { useClassroomLayout } from "@/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout";
import { useClassroomHome } from "./useClassroomHome";
import ClassroomHomeView from "./ClassroomHomeView";
import "./home.css";

/**
 * Classroom Home. Reads the student's own record and renders the dashboard;
 * every route it opens already exists in the rail, so this page is a shortcut
 * into the classroom rather than a second navigation.
 */
export default function ClassroomHome() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { openSection } = useClassroomLayout();
  const { model, loading, markDone, clearMessage, completingKey } = useClassroomHome();

  return (
    <ClassroomHomeView
      model={model}
      loading={loading}
      onOpen={(route) => navigate(route)}
      onSeeAllWork={() => openSection("work")}
      onMarkDone={markDone}
      onClearMessage={async (conversationId) => {
        try {
          await clearMessage(conversationId);
        } catch {
          toast.error("Could not clear that message. Try again.");
        }
      }}
      completingKey={completingKey}
    />
  );
}

  const { user } = useAuth();
  const permissions = useUserPermissions();
  const { displayName } = useClassroomLayout();

  const [completingKey, setCompletingKey] = React.useState<string | null>(null);


  // Mirrors the rail's own rule, so a door on Home and the same door in the
  // rail can never disagree: admins pass everything, an item with no flags is
  // open to any signed-in student, otherwise any one flag is enough.
  const canAccess = React.useCallback(
    (...flags: PermissionFlag[]) =>
      permissions.is_admin || flags.length === 0 || flags.some((flag) => permissions[flag]),
    [permissions],
  );

  const access = React.useMemo(
    () => ({
      messages: canAccess("has_chat_access"),
      schedule: canAccess("has_schedule_access"),
      activities: canAccess("has_activity_access"),
      drill: canAccess("has_drill_access"),
    }),
    [canAccess],
  );

  const itemsQuery = useClassroomItems();
  const bookingQuery = useStudentBooking();
  const planQuery = useCurrentWeeklyPlan();
  const { conversations, clearConversationFromHome } = useInbox();

  // Same key and same fetcher as the todo feed, so this is a cache read rather
  // than a second round trip. The feed flattens activities to a title and a
  // subtitle; the hero needs the authored tagline and the item count too.
  const { data: activities = [] } = useQuery<ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>({
    queryKey: ["todo-feed:activity", user?.id],
    enabled: !!user,
    staleTime: 30_000,
    queryFn: async () => listStudentAssignments(user!.id),
  });

  // Every label on this page is a day-granularity comparison ("due today", "in
  // four days"), so the clock is re-read once per calendar day rather than once
  // per render. A fresh Date each render would invalidate every memo below and
  // buy nothing.
  const today = localDayISO();
  // `today` is the point of the dependency, not an accident: it is the only
  // thing that should make the page re-read the clock. The rule cannot see that.
  // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  const now = React.useMemo(() => new Date(), [today]);

  /** Due dates live on classroom_items; the feed rows carry only their source id. */
  const dueBySourceId = React.useMemo(() => {
    const map = new Map<string, string>();
    for (const item of itemsQuery.data ?? []) {
      if (item.due_at && item.source_id) map.set(item.source_id, item.due_at);

import { useOrientation, OrientationCardView } from "@/components/classroom/OrientationCard";
import AnnouncementBanner from "@/components/classroom/AnnouncementBanner";
import ClassroomHome from "@/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHome";

/**
 * The classroom home: the student dashboard.
 *
 * One screen that answers, in order, what to do now, what is assigned, whether
 * the coach said anything, when the next session is, and what the week holds.
 *
 * Except before setup is finished. Then the intake owns the whole stage: a
 * student answering questions about their week should not be reading a week
 * they have not built yet, and the old split showed both at once. They begin
 * from the invitation, walk the questions one at a time, and the classroom
 * appears behind them when they are done.
 */
export default function Classroom() {
  const { loaded, completed, setCompleted, name, answers, save } = useOrientation();

  // Nothing until the profile read lands: drawing the home first and swapping
  // it for the intake a beat later is worse than a moment of quiet.
  if (!loaded) return null;

  if (!completed) {
    return (
      <OrientationCardView
        initialName={name}
        initialAnswers={answers}
        onSave={save}
        onDone={() => setCompleted(true)}
        startCollapsed
      />
    );
  }

  return (
    <>
      <AnnouncementBanner />
      <ClassroomHome />
    </>
  );
}

    return <>{children}</>;
  }

  // Only a true cold read blocks; cached reads and background refetches keep
  // this false, so gated navigations do not flash.
  if (permissions.loading) {
    return (
      <div className="min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center">
        <div className="w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin" />
      </div>
    );
  }

  if (requireAdmin) {
    return permissions.is_admin ? <>{children}</> : <LockedModule />;
  }

  if (allowSignedIn) {
    return <>{children}</>;
  }

{"rows":[{"def":"CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_profile_admin_fields()\n RETURNS trigger\n LANGUAGE plpgsql\n SET search_path TO 'public'\nAS $function$\nBEGIN\n  IF auth.uid() IS NULL OR public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin') THEN\n    RETURN NEW;\n  END IF;\n\n  IF NEW.has_bootcamp_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_bootcamp_access  OR\n     NEW.has_classroom_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_classroom_access OR\n     NEW.has_analytics_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_analytics_access OR\n     NEW.has_schedule_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_schedule_access  OR\n     NEW.has_practice_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_practice_access  OR\n     NEW.has_drill_access     IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_drill_access     OR\n     NEW.has_waj_access       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_waj_access       OR\n     NEW.has_journal_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_journal_access   OR\n     NEW.has_flagged_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_flagged_access   OR\n     NEW.has_chat_access      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_chat_access      OR\n     NEW.has_export_access    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_export_access    OR\n     NEW.has_ta_access        IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_ta_access        OR\n     NEW.proficiency_level    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.proficiency_level    OR\n     NEW.class_id             IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.class_id             THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'profiles: access flags, proficiency_level and class_id are admin-controlled';\n  END IF;\n\n  RETURN NEW;\nEND;\n$function$\n"}]}

      // Apply a template (explicit flag map) to a user. One DB update so
      // the user never sits in a partially-applied state.
      if (action === "apply_template") {
        const { class_id, flags } = body;
        if (!class_id || !flags || typeof flags !== "object") {
          return jsonRes({ error: "Invalid params" }, 400);
        }
        const updates: Record<string, boolean> = {};
        for (const flag of ALL_FLAGS) {
          if (typeof flags[flag] === "boolean") {
            updates[flag] = flags[flag];
          }
        }
        if (Object.keys(updates).length === 0) {
          return jsonRes({ error: "No valid flags" }, 400);
        }
        const { error } = await admin
          .from("profiles")
          .update(updates)
          .eq("class_id", class_id);
        if (error) throw error;
        return jsonRes({ success: true });
      }

export const RAIL_CATALOG: RailItem[] = [
  { id: "home", label: "Home", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], allowSignedIn: true, required: true },
  { id: "drill", label: "Drill", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_drill_access"] },
  // The Testing Centre is where a student sits something under test conditions:
  // one real timed section, or a full practice test. It is deliberately its own
  // rail entry rather than a tab inside Drill — drilling is practice you stop
  // and think inside, and a sitting is not, so putting them behind one door
  // invites starting a 35:00 clock by accident.
  { id: "testing", label: "Testing Centre", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_drill_access"] },
  { id: "work", label: "Assigned work", group: "classroom", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], section: true },
  { id: "notes", label: "Notes and recaps", group: "study", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], section: true },
  { id: "bootcamps", label: "Bootcamps", group: "study", flags: ["has_bootcamp_access"] },
  { id: "activities", label: "Activities", group: "study", flags: ["has_activity_access"] },
  { id: "review", label: "Review", group: "review", flags: ["has_journal_access", "has_waj_access", "has_flagged_access"], section: true },
  { id: "analytics", label: "Analytics", group: "review", flags: ["has_analytics_access"] },
  // Reference, not a paid feature: what the schools a student is aiming at
  // actually admitted. Open to anyone signed in, like Home.
  { id: "admissions", label: "Admissions", group: "review", flags: ["has_classroom_access"], allowSignedIn: true },
  { id: "history", label: "History", group: "review", flags: ["has_analytics_access"] },
  { id: "messages", label: "Messages", group: "support", flags: ["has_chat_access"] },
  // Schedule owns booking too: one rail entry, two stage tabs (Schedule | Book
  // a session). Two adjacent Support entries for the same errand opened
  // unrelated-looking screens, and the empty Schedule had no way to book at all.
  { id: "schedule", label: "Schedule", group: "support", flags: ["has_schedule_access"], allowSignedIn: true },
];

Tool result:
  // One button, whole account: revoking shuts every door except the classroom
  // home page, so the student can still sign in and land somewhere truthful.
  // Granting opens everything back up.
  const setAllAccess = async (person: PeopleRow, grant: boolean) => {
    const who = person.display_name || person.email;
    if (!grant && !window.confirm(
      `Revoke all access for ${who}?\n\n` +
      'They keep their login and their home page. Everything else — drill, ' +
      'practice, bootcamps, review, messages, schedule — is locked.',
    )) return;

    const flags: Record<string, boolean> = {};
    for (const key of ALL_FLAG_KEYS) flags[key] = grant;
    flags.has_classroom_access = true;

    const previous = users;
    setUsers((prev) => prev.map((user) => (user.class_id === person.class_id ? { ...user, ...flags } : user)));
    try {
      const res = await supabase.functions.invoke('admin-manage-users', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: { action: 'apply_template', class_id: person.class_id, flags },
      });
      if (res.error) throw res.error;
      toast.success(grant ? `${who} has full access` : `${who} is locked to their home page`);
      await fetchData();
    } catch (error) {
      setUsers(previous);
      console.error('setAllAccess failed', { class_id: person.class_id, grant, error });
      toast.error('Failed to update access');
    }
  };

    setCompleted: vi.fn(),
    name: "Ada",
    answers: {},
    save: vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(true),
  },
}));

vi.mock("@/components/classroom/OrientationCard", () => ({
  useOrientation: () => orientation,
  OrientationCardView: () => <div>Set up your classroom</div>,
}));
vi.mock("@/components/classroom/AnnouncementBanner", () => ({
  default: () => <div>Announcement</div>,
}));
vi.mock("@/components/classroom/home/ClassroomHome", () => ({
  default: () => <div>Your week</div>,
}));

describe("Classroom", () => {
  it("gives the whole stage to the intake until it is finished", () => {
    render(<Classroom />);
    expect(screen.getByText("Set up your classroom")).toBeTruthy();
    // The home used to sit underneath the intake, so a student answering
    // questions about their week was reading a week they had not built.
    expect(screen.queryByText("Your week")).toBeNull();
    expect(screen.queryByText("Announcement")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("draws the classroom once setup is done", () => {
    orientation.completed = true;
    render(<Classroom />);
    expect(screen.getByText("Your week")).toBeTruthy();
    expect(screen.queryByText("Set up your classroom")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("draws nothing until the profile read lands", () => {
    orientation.loaded = false;
    const { container } = render(<Classroom />);
    expect(container.textContent).toBe("");
  });
});

    if (!userId) return;

          schema: "public",

Without this, every gated page would hold its own socket.\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!authReady || !userId) return;\n    const entry = acquireProfileWatch(userId, () => {\n      void queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\"user-permissions\", userId] });\n    });\n    return () => releaseProfileWatch(userId, entry);\n  }, [authReady, userId, queryClient]);\n\n  const query = useQuery<PermissionFlags>({''')\ns=s.replace('''    queryKey: [\"user-permissions\", user?.id],\n    enabled: authReady && !!user,\n    staleTime: 5 * 60_000,\n    gcTime: 30 * 60_000,''','''    queryKey: [\"user-permissions\", userId],\n    enabled: authReady && !!user,\n    // Realtime (below) is the primary path for a revoke to land; the short\n    // stale window and the focus refetch are the fallback when the socket is\n    // down, so \"Revoke All Access\" is never more than a tab switch away from\n    // taking effect. Cached data keeps `loading` false, so neither flashes.\n    staleTime: 60_000,\n    gcTime: 30 * 60_000,\n    refetchOnWindowFocus: true,''')\ns=s.replace('''class SessionExpiredError extends Error {}\n''','''class SessionExpiredError extends Error {}\n\n// One realtime channel per signed-in user, shared by every mounted consumer.\n// An UPDATE to the student's own profiles row (an admin flipping a flag)\n// invalidates the cached permissions, so a revoke takes effect on the next\n// render rather than when the stale window runs out. RLS scopes the stream to\n// the student's own row; profiles is in the supabase_realtime publication\n// (migration 20260922120000).\ntype ProfileWatch = { channel: ReturnType<typeof supabase.channel>; listeners: Set<() => void> };\nconst profileWatches = new Map<string, ProfileWatch>();\n\nfunction acquireProfileWatch(userId: string, onChange: () => void): () => void {\n  let watch = profileWatches.get(userId);\n  if (!watch) {\n    const listeners = new Set<() => void>();\n    const channel = supabase\n      .channel(`profile-access-${userId}`)\n      .on(\n        \"postgres_changes\",\n        { event: \"UPDATE\", schema: \"public\", table: \"profiles\", filter: `class_id=eq.${userId}` },\n        () => listeners.forEach((fn) => fn()),\n      )\n      .subscribe();\n    watch = { channel, listeners };\n    profileWatches.set(userId, watch);\n  }\n  watch.listeners.add(onChange);\n  return onChange;\n}\n\nfunction releaseProfileWatch(userId: string, listener: () => void) {\n  const watch = profileWatches.get(userId);\n  if (!watch) return;\n  watch.listeners.delete(listener);\n  if (watch.listeners.size === 0) {\n    profileWatches.delete(userId);\n    void supabase.removeChannel(watch.channel);\n  }\n}\n''')\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)\nEOF\ncat > supabase/migrations/20260922120000_profiles_realtime_and_activity_guard.sql <<'EOF'\n-- \"Revoke All Access\" has to land while the student is still signed in.\n-- useUserPermissions caches the flags client-side; it now subscribes to the\n-- student's own profiles row and refetches on UPDATE, which needs the table in\n-- the realtime publication. RLS still applies to the stream, so a student only\n-- ever sees changes to their own row.\nDO $$\nBEGIN\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_publication_tables\n    WHERE pubname = 'supabase_realtime' AND schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'profiles'\n  ) THEN\n    ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE public.profiles;\n  END IF;\nEND $$;\n\n-- has_activity_access was added after guard_profile_admin_fields() was written,\n-- so it was the one flag a student could flip on their own row through the\n-- \"Students can update own profile\" policy. Close it.\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_profile_admin_fields()\n RETURNS trigger\n LANGUAGE plpgsql\n SET search_path TO 'public'\nAS $function$\nBEGIN\n  IF auth.uid() IS NULL OR public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin') THEN\n    RETURN NEW;\n  END IF;\n\n  IF NEW.has_bootcamp_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_bootcamp_access  OR\n     NEW.has_classroom_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_classroom_access OR\n     NEW.has_analytics_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_analytics_access OR\n     NEW.has_schedule_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_schedule_access  OR\n     NEW.has_practice_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_practice_access  OR\n     NEW.has_drill_access     IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_drill_access     OR\n     NEW.has_waj_access       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_waj_access       OR\n     NEW.has_journal_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_journal_access   OR\n     NEW.has_flagged_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_flagged_access   OR\n     NEW.has_chat_access      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_chat_access      OR\n     NEW.has_export_access    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_export_access    OR\n     NEW.has_ta_access        IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_ta_access        OR\n     NEW.has_activity_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_activity_access  OR\n     NEW.proficiency_level    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.proficiency_level    OR\n     NEW.class_id             IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.class_id             THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'profiles: access flags, proficiency_level and class_id are admin-controlled';\n  END IF;\n\n  RETURN NEW;\nEND;\n$function$;\nEOF\necho ok", "description": "Add realtime permission refresh and the migration for publication and trigger guard"}

One shared channel per user, not one per mounted route.\n- `LockedModule` gets a revoked state. \"Back to your classroom\" would have looped a revoked student onto the same screen, so it is not offered; the copy becomes \"Your access is paused\".\n\n## Verification\n\n- `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean.\n- Full suite: 4720 passed, same 11 files / 13 tests failing as `origin/main` (switchyard, activity-centre, booking, AssistantComposer, singleSessionCore). Baseline confirmed by running the suite on `origin/main` in the same worktree. No new failures.\n- New tests: revoked Classroom shows the locked notice, admin still sees the home, revoked student gets the paused copy with no classroom loop, and the revoke payload asserts `has_classroom_access: false`.\n\n## Note before merge\n\nThe migration has to reach the live database for the realtime invalidation to work. Backend is Lovable Cloud-managed, so that deploy is yours to trigger. Without it the fallback still applies: a revoke lands within 60 seconds or on the next tab focus.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3", "description": "Open the pull request", "timeout": 300000}

## assistant

You were right that it didn't actually revoke everything. It left six doors open. All are closed now.

**PR:** [vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db#129](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/129)

## What a "revoked" student could still do

| Gap | What stayed reachable |
|---|---|
| `has_classroom_access` was deliberately kept on | Assigned work, notes and recaps, item pages, TA assignments, uploads |
| `/guided/:assignmentId` and `/guided-drills/:slug` had **no guard at all** | Every guided-drill link they'd ever been sent still ran |
| `/conditional-indicators-refresher` admitted any signed-in user | Teaching content |
| `/classroom/uploads` had no guard | Handed-in and returned work |
| `has_activity_access` was missing from the DB trigger | A student could turn Activities back on themselves, since RLS lets them update their own profile row |
| Flags cached 5 minutes, never invalidated | A revoke took up to 5 minutes to bite on an open tab |

The first one was the big one. The old comment said it was intentional so the student "still has somewhere to stand" — but the classroom home *is* the coursework, so that reasoning kept the main room open.

## What it does now

Revoke turns off all 13 flags. The `/classroom` route still lets them land so they don't hit a redirect loop, but the page itself shows "Your access is paused" instead of their dashboard. Their login and all their work survive untouched.

I also fixed a dead end I created: "Back to your classroom" on the locked screen would have looped a revoked student straight back onto the same screen, so that button is hidden for them. If messages are off too, the screen just tells them to reach out to you.

Grant Full Access is unchanged and turns everything back on.

## Verified

Typecheck clean, build clean. Full suite: 4720 passing. There are 13 pre-existing failures (switchyard, activity-centre, booking, AssistantComposer) — I confirmed those by running the suite on a clean `origin/main` in the same worktree first, and they fail identically there. None are mine.

**What I did not verify:** the student-side locked screen in a real browser. That needs signing in as a revoked student, and I won't enter someone's password. The behavior is covered by tests instead — revoked student sees the locked notice, admin still sees the home, the revoke payload sends `has_classroom_access: false`.

## One thing needs you

The migration (`has_activity_access` trigger fix + putting `profiles` in the realtime publication) has to reach the live database. Backend is Lovable Cloud-managed, so per your rules that deploy is your call, not mine. Want me to send it to the Lovable agent?

Without it, the code still works — a revoke lands within 60 seconds or on the next tab focus rather than instantly, and the `has_activity_access` self-grant hole stays open until the trigger updates.

RLS still applies to the stream, so a student only\\\\n-- ever sees changes to their own row.\\\\nDO $$\\\\nBEGIN\\\\n  IF NOT EXISTS (\\\\n    SELECT 1 FROM pg_publication_tables\\\\n    WHERE pubname = 'supabase_realtime' AND schemaname = 'public' AND tablename = 'profiles'\\\\n  ) THEN\\\\n    ALTER PUBLICATION supabase_realtime ADD TABLE public.profiles;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\nEND $$;\\\\n\\\\n-- has_activity_access was added after guard_profile_admin_fields() was written,\\\\n-- so it was the one flag a student could flip on their own row through the\\\\n-- \\\\\\\"Students can update own profile\\\\\\\" policy. Close it.\\\\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_profile_admin_fields()\\\\n RETURNS trigger\\\\n LANGUAGE plpgsql\\\\n SET search_path TO 'public'\\\\nAS $function$\\\\nBEGIN\\\\n  IF auth.uid() IS NULL OR public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin') THEN\\\\n    RETURN NEW;\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  IF NEW.has_bootcamp_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_bootcamp_access  OR\\\\n     NEW.has_classroom_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_classroom_access OR\\\\n     NEW.has_analytics_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_analytics_access OR\\\\n     NEW.has_schedule_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_schedule_access  OR\\\\n     NEW.has_practice_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_practice_access  OR\\\\n     NEW.has_drill_access     IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_drill_access     OR\\\\n     NEW.has_waj_access       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_waj_access       OR\\\\n     NEW.has_journal_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_journal_access   OR\\\\n     NEW.has_flagged_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_flagged_access   OR\\\\n     NEW.has_chat_access      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_chat_access      OR\\\\n     NEW.has_export_access    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_export_access    OR\\\\n     NEW.has_ta_access        IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_ta_access        OR\\\\n     NEW.has_activity_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_activity_access  OR\\\\n     NEW.proficiency_level    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.proficiency_level    OR\\\\n     NEW.class_id             IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.class_id             THEN\\\\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'profiles: access flags, proficiency_level and class_id are admin-controlled';\\\\n  END IF;\\\\n\\\\n  RETURN NEW;\\\\nEND;\\\\n$function$;\\\"}\">\n</lov-tool-use>\n\n<lov-tool-use id=\"thinking-\" name=\"lov-think\" data=\"Applied. ## Two things still open

**1. The 14 revoked students are still not actually revoked.** The code fix changes what the button does from now on; it doesn't retroactively fix rows already written. All 14 still have `has_classroom_access = true`, so they still have the classroom home, assigned work, notes and recaps, uploads, and any guided-drill link they were sent. I can close them out either by you clicking Revoke All Access on each, or with one UPDATE setting `has_classroom_access = false` for exactly those 14. I didn't do it on my own — that's 14 real students losing access and it should be your call. Say the word and I'll run it.

**2. The frontend fix is not live yet.** Merging to main builds a preview; only a publish reaches lsatprep.study. So right now the database half is live but the app half isn't — meaning the button still keeps classroom on until you publish. Want me to run the Lovable deploy?

The worktree is at `projects/lsatu-wt-revoke` if you want to look at anything; I can remove it whenever.

## assistant

Deleting the auth user cascades to about 60 tables. That includes things you may not want gone: `student_sessions` (your session records), `attempts`, journals, RC annotations, transcripts. Meanwhile `booking_passes` and `booking_commerce_events` are SET NULL, so paid-pass and payment rows would survive but become orphaned with no student attached.

That's a big enough fork that I want your call before building.

      const mode = url.searchParams.get("mode");

      // Analytics mode
      if (mode === "analytics") {
        const { data: profiles } = await admin
          .from("profiles")
          .select("class_id, last_seen_at, " + ALL_FLAGS.join(", "));

        const { data: { users: authUsers } } = await admin.auth.admin.listUsers({ perPage: 1000 });

        const now = new Date();
        const dayAgo = new Date(now.getTime() - 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
        const weekAgo = new Date(now.getTime() - 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);

        const { data: roles } = await admin.from("user_roles").select("user_id, role");
        const adminCount = (roles || []).filter((r: any) => r.role === "admin").length;

        const activeToday = (profiles || []).filter((p: any) =>
          p.last_seen_at && new Date(p.last_seen_at) > dayAgo
        ).length;

        const activeWeek = (profiles || []).filter((p: any) =>
          p.last_seen_at && new Date(p.last_seen_at) > weekAgo
        ).length;

        // Feature adoption counts
        const adoption: Record<string, number> = {};
        for (const flag of ALL_FLAGS) {
          adoption[flag] = (profiles || []).filter((p: any) => p[flag] === true).length;
        }

        // Recent attempts stats
        const { count: totalAttempts } = await admin
          .from("attempts")
          .select("id", { count: "exact", head: true });

        const { count: weekAttempts } = await admin
          .from("attempts")
          .select("id", { count: "exact", head: true })
          .gte("timestamp_iso", weekAgo.toISOString());

        return jsonRes({
          total_users: authUsers?.length || 0,
          active_today: activeToday,
          active_week: activeWeek,
          admin_count: adminCount,
          feature_adoption: adoption,
          total_attempts: totalAttempts || 0,
          week_attempts: weekAttempts || 0,
        });
      }

      // Default: list users
      const { data: profiles, error: pErr } = await admin
        .from("profiles")
        .select("class_id, display_name, last_seen_at, " + ALL_FLAGS.join(", "));
      if (pErr) throw pErr;

      const { data: { users: authUsers }, error: aErr } = await admin.auth.admin.listUsers({ perPage: 1000 });
      if (aErr) throw aErr;

      const emailMap = new Map(authUsers.map((u: any) => [u.id, u.email]));

      const { data: roles } = await admin.from("user_roles").select("user_id, role");
      const roleMap = new Map<string, string>();
      for (const r of roles || []) {
        roleMap.set(r.user_id, r.role);
      }

      const result = (profiles || []).map((p: any) => ({
        ...p,
        email: emailMap.get(p.class_id) || "unknown",
        role: roleMap.get(p.class_id) || "user",
      }));

      return jsonRes(result);
    }

{"command": "cat > supabase/migrations/20260922140000_student_archive_and_delete.sql <<'EOF'\n-- Two ways to remove a student from the People page.\n--\n-- Archive is the everyday one: access off, hidden from the list, every record\n-- kept, reversible. It is a profiles column, not a deletion.\n--\n-- Hard delete removes the auth user, which cascades to roughly sixty tables.\n-- Two things must outlive it: Joshua's own session records, and the payment\n-- and booking history. The booking tables already snapshot email and name, so\n-- they read fine once detached. student_sessions did not: student_id was NOT\n-- NULL and cascaded, so deleting a student destroyed the teaching record of\n-- sessions he taught. That is his work product, not theirs.\n\n-- ── Archive ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nALTER TABLE public.profiles\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS archived_at timestamptz,\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS archived_reason text;\n\nCOMMENT ON COLUMN public.profiles.archived_at IS\n  'Set when a student is archived: access revoked, hidden from the People list, records kept. NULL means active.';\n\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS profiles_archived_at_idx\n  ON public.profiles (archived_at) WHERE archived_at IS NOT NULL;\n\n-- Archiving is an admin act, like the access flags, so students cannot clear\n-- it on their own row through the \"Students can update own profile\" policy.\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_profile_admin_fields()\n RETURNS trigger\n LANGUAGE plpgsql\n SET search_path TO 'public'\nAS $function$\nBEGIN\n  IF auth.uid() IS NULL OR public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin') THEN\n    RETURN NEW;\n  END IF;\n\n  IF NEW.has_bootcamp_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_bootcamp_access  OR\n     NEW.has_classroom_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_classroom_access OR\n     NEW.has_analytics_access IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_analytics_access OR\n     NEW.has_schedule_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_schedule_access  OR\n     NEW.has_practice_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_practice_access  OR\n     NEW.has_drill_access     IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_drill_access     OR\n     NEW.has_waj_access       IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_waj_access       OR\n     NEW.has_journal_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_journal_access   OR\n     NEW.has_flagged_access   IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_flagged_access   OR\n     NEW.has_chat_access      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_chat_access      OR\n     NEW.has_export_access    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_export_access    OR\n     NEW.has_ta_access        IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_ta_access        OR\n     NEW.has_activity_access  IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.has_activity_access  OR\n     NEW.archived_at          IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.archived_at          OR\n     NEW.archived_reason      IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.archived_reason      OR\n     NEW.proficiency_level    IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.proficiency_level    OR\n     NEW.class_id             IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.class_id             THEN\n    RAISE EXCEPTION 'profiles: access flags, archive state, proficiency_level and class_id are admin-controlled';\n  END IF;\n\n  RETURN NEW;\nEND;\n$function$;\n\n-- ── Who was deleted ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n-- The surviving rows keep the old uuid in former_student_id, which is a plain\n-- column and not a foreign key. This table is what makes that uuid readable\n-- again, so a detached session or payment still has a name on it.\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS public.deleted_students (\n  user_id      uuid PRIMARY KEY,\n  email        text,\n  display_name text,\n  deleted_at   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),\n  deleted_by   uuid,\n  note         text\n);\n\nALTER TABLE public.deleted_students ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;\n\nDROP POLICY IF EXISTS \"Admins can view deleted students\" ON public.deleted_students;\nCREATE POLICY \"Admins can view deleted students\"\n  ON public.deleted_students FOR SELECT\n  USING (public.has_role(auth.uid(), 'admin'));\n\n-- ── Keep the session record ──────────────────────────────────────────────\nALTER TABLE public.student_sessions\n  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS former_student_id uuid;\n\nALTER TABLE public.student_sessions ALTER COLUMN student_id DROP NOT NULL;\n\nDO $$\nDECLARE fk_name text;\nBEGIN\n  SELECT c.conname INTO fk_name\n  FROM pg_constraint c\n  JOIN unnest(c.conkey) k(attnum) ON true\n  JOIN pg_attribute a ON a.attrelid = c.conrelid AND a.attnum = k.attnum\n  WHERE c.contype = 'f'\n    AND c.conrelid = 'public.student_sessions'::regclass\n    AND c.confrelid = 'auth.users'::regclass\n    AND a.attname = 'student_id'\n  LIMIT 1;\n\n  IF fk_name IS NOT NULL THEN\n    EXECUTE format('ALTER TABLE public.student_sessions DROP CONSTRAINT %I', fk_name);\n  END IF;\n\n  ALTER TABLE public.student_sessions\n    ADD CONSTRAINT student_sessions_student_id_fkey\n    FOREIGN KEY (student_id) REFERENCES auth.users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL;\nEND $$;\n\n-- The booking and payment tables are already ON DELETE SET NULL and already\n-- carry their own email/name snapshots. They only need the old id kept so the\n-- rows can still be grouped per person after the account is gone.\nALTER TABLE public.booking_passes          ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS former_student_id uuid;\nALTER TABLE public.booking_commerce_events ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS former_student_id uuid;\nALTER TABLE public.single_session_bookings ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS former_student_id uuid;\nALTER TABLE public.external_booking_events ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS former_student_id uuid;\n\n-- ── Snapshot on the way out ──────────────────────────────────────────────\n-- Runs BEFORE the delete, so it can still read the profile and still see the\n-- rows that are about to be detached by the SET NULL cascades.\nCREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.snapshot_deleted_student()\n RETURNS trigger\n LANGUAGE plpgsql\n SECURITY DEFINER\n SET search_path TO 'public'\nAS $function$\nBEGIN\n  INSERT INTO public.deleted_students (user_id, email, display_name, deleted_at)\n  VALUES (\n    OLD.id,\n    OLD.email,\n    (SELECT display_name FROM public.profiles WHERE class_id = OLD.id::text),\n    now()\n  )\n  ON CONFLICT (user_id) DO UPDATE\n    SET email = EXCLUDED.email,\n        display_name =       }

      // Set role
      if (action === "set_role") {
        const { user_id, role } = body;
        if (!user_id || !["admin", "user"].includes(role)) {
          return jsonRes({ error: "Invalid params" }, 400);
        }
        // Protect the primary admin: it can never be demoted, by anyone
        // (including itself). This guarantees the system keeps at least one
        // admin and cannot be locked out.
        if (role === "user") {
          const { data: target } = await admin.auth.admin.getUserById(user_id);
          if ((target?.user?.email || "").toLowerCase() === PRIMARY_ADMIN_EMAIL) {
            return jsonRes({ error: "The primary admin account cannot be demoted." }, 403);
          }
        }
        if (role === "admin") {
          const { error: upsertError } = await admin
            .from("user_roles")
            .upsert({ user_id, role: "admin" }, { onConflict: "user_id,role" });
          if (upsertError) {
            console.error("set_role: upsert failed", { user_id, error: upsertError });
            throw upsertError;
          }
        } else {
          const { error: deleteError } = await admin
            .from("user_roles")
            .delete()
            .eq("user_id", user_id)
            .eq("role", "admin");
          if (deleteError) {
            console.error("set_role: delete failed", { user_id, error: deleteError });
            throw deleteError;
          }
        }
        return jsonRes({ success: true });
      }

      return jsonRes({ error: "Unknown action" }, 400);
    }

    return jsonRes({ error: "Method not allowed" }, 405);
  } catch (err) {

  const resetOnboarding = async (person: PeopleRow) => {

    if (!window.confirm(

      'Clears their setup answers and name, re-locks access, and lets the ' +
      'intake run again. Their login and their practice history are untouched.',
    )) return;

    try {
      const res = await supabase.functions.invoke('admin-manage-users', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: { action: 'reset_onboarding', class_id: person.class_id },
      });
      if (res.error) throw res.error;
      toast.success(`${who} is back to a new account`);
      await fetchData();
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('resetOnboarding failed', { class_id: person.class_id, error });
      toast.error('Failed to reset the account');
    }
  };

          <RotateCcw className="h-3.5 w-3.5" strokeWidth={1.5} />
          Reset to a new account
        </button>
        <p className="mt-2 text-xs leading-relaxed text-black/45">
          Clears their setup answers and name, re-locks access, and lets the intake
          run again. The login and their practice history survive.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

      <div className="mb-2 flex items-center gap-2 text-black/45">
        {icon}
        <span className="text-[10px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-[0.18em]">{label}</span>

  toast: {

=====TAIL=====
    // The simplified People page only manages Drill access plus role. No
    // broad permission matrix, templates, or bulk grant/revoke.
    expect(screen.queryByText('Templates')).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText('Role Management')).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText('Protected primary admin')).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it('shows one whole-account access control per student', async () => {

    expect(screen.queryByRole('switch', { name: 'Bootcamps' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByRole('switch', { name: 'Classroom' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByRole('switch', { name: 'Practice' })).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it('revokes every feature except the classroom home page', async () => {
    vi.spyOn(window, 'confirm').mockReturnValue(true);

});

function renderPeople() {
  const queryClient = new QueryClient({ defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: false }, mutations: { retry: false } } });
  // Roster names are links into the student dossier (T3.2), so the surface needs
  // a router in scope.
  return render(
    <MemoryRouter>
      <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
        <DeskPeopleSurface />
      </QueryClientProvider>
    </MemoryRouter>,
  );
}

const users = [
  {
    class_id: 'student-1',
    email: 'meghan@example.com',
    display_name: 'Meghan Mullins',
    role: 'user',
    last_seen_at: '2026-06-18T12:58:00.000Z',
    has_bootcamp_access: true,
    has_classroom_access: true,
    has_analytics_access: true,
    has_schedule_access: true,
    has_practice_access: true,
    has_drill_access: true,
    has_waj_access: true,
    has_flagged_access: true,
    has_chat_access: true,
    has_export_access: false,
    has_ta_access: false,
  },
  {
    class_id: 'admin-1',
    email: 'contact@aspiringattorneys.com',
    display_name: 'Joshua',
    role: 'admin',
    last_seen_at: '2026-06-18T12:00:00.000Z',
    has_bootcamp_access: true,
    has_classroom_access: true,
    has_analytics_access: true,
    has_schedule_access: true,
    has_practice_access: true,
    has_drill_access: true,
    has_waj_access: true,
    has_flagged_access: true,
    has_chat_access: true,
    has_export_access: true,
    has_ta_access: true,
  },
];

describe('DeskPeopleSurface', () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    vi.setSystemTime(new Date('2026-06-18T13:00:00.000Z'));
    invokeMock.mockClear();
    getSessionMock.mockResolvedValue({ data: { session: { user: { id: 'admin-1' } } } });
    invokeMock.mockImplementation((_name, options) => {
      if (options?.headers?.['x-mode'] === 'analytics') {
        return Promise.resolve({
          data: {
            total_users: 2,
            active_today: 2,
            active_week: 2,
            admin_count: 1,
            feature_adoption: {},
            total_attempts: 0,
            week_attempts: 0,
          },
          error: null,
        });
      }
      if (options?.method === 'POST') {
        const { class_id, field, value } = options.body as Record<string, unknown>;
        return Promise.resolve({
          data: { success: true, profile: { class_id, [field as string]: value } },
          error: null,
        });
      }
      return Promise.resolve({ data: users, error: null });
    });
  });

## assistant

Built and merged to main ([#130](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/130), `dbeeac5`). You now have two ways to remove someone, deliberately different in weight.

## Archive — the everyday one

In a student's expanded panel, under "Remove from your list." Turns off all 13 flags, takes them off the list *and* the header count, keeps every record, and lands on their open tab right away. An **Archived** filter chip shows them when you want them.

Unarchive brings them back with access still off, so you choose what to reopen rather than silently restoring all 13 doors.

## Delete permanently — the exception

Red button below it. You have to type their email exactly; a mismatch does nothing. It destroys the account, practice history, journals, flagged questions, and assigned work.

Two things survive, per your call:

- **Your session records.** This was a real trap: `student_sessions.student_id` was NOT NULL and cascaded, so deleting a student would have wiped the teaching record of sessions *you* taught. Now it detaches instead.
- **Payment and booking history.** Those tables already stored email and name, so they still read properly. They now keep the old ID too, so rows stay groupable per person.

A trigger records who was deleted into a new `deleted_students` table before the cascade fires, so a detached session or payment still has a name against it rather than a bare NULL.

Guards: primary admin can't be deleted or archived, an admin can't delete themselves, and students can't unarchive themselves.

**One thing I caught mid-build and want to flag:** I had written an `auth.admin.signOut(class_id)` call into the archive path. Checking the signature, that API takes a JWT, not a user ID — it would have silently failed on every archive while the UI claimed "signs them out." I removed it and corrected the copy. The realtime subscription is what actually makes an archive bite immediately.

Verified: typecheck, `deno check`, and build all clean; full suite 4724 passing with only the same pre-existing failures as main. New tests cover the archived filter, the archive call, and delete refusing a mismatched email.

## Three things need you now

Nothing from either session is live on lsatprep.study yet.

1. **This migration** needs applying to the database. New credit spend, so I'm not sending it without you saying so.
2. **The frontend publish** is still outstanding from the revoke work. Until it runs, neither the revoke fix nor archive/delete exists for you in the browser.
3. **Those 14 students** are still not actually revoked — they keep `has_classroom_access = true` from before the fix. Now that archive exists, archiving them is probably the cleaner move than re-revoking, since it also clears them off your list.

Want me to do all three?

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\grill-me

# Grill Me

Two layers. The **interview method** comes from the `grilling` skill and is not restated here. The **capture discipline** below is what this wrapper adds: every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up.

If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.

## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)

1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.

## Step 2: run the interview

Call the Skill tool with "grilling". Follow it exactly: map the design tree, ask the whole frontier as a numbered round with a recommended answer per question, dispatch sub-agents for facts instead of asking the user, wait for answers, recompute the frontier, repeat until it is empty.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:

- Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning stays traceable.
- Read back the saved entries before continuing. If the write fails, say so, keep the answers visible in chat, and do not claim they were saved or collect more answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next round.

Never let two rounds pile up in your head before writing. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, your recommendations, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Round 1
#### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Recommended: {your recommendation, labeled}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts and decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
#### Q2 - ...
### Round 2
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause

Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Near the end, offer a completeness backstop ("anything we haven't touched?"). Then:

- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and your recommendations stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan or design interview without a request to update canonical context, the raw capture is the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap: clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, next step or resume point.

Both lookups are running. Here's the frontier that doesn't depend on them.

❓ **Q1** — **What a tier actually is**: Today the app enforces access through 13 boolean columns on `profiles`, and everything reads them — `ProtectedRoute`, the classroom rail, the Home dashboard. A tier could either (a) be a label that *computes* those 13 flags, leaving the flags as the enforcement layer, or (b) *replace* the flags with a single `access_tier` column that every gate checks instead.

Option (b) is cleaner on paper but means touching every gate in the app, and it can't express Tier 2 at all, since Tier 2 is by definition "these specific features and not those."

➡️ **(a) Tier computes the flags.** Add one `access_tier` column that records *why* a student's flags look the way they do. Tier 1 sets all 13 on, Tier 2 is whatever you toggle, Tier 3 sets them off and leans on assignment. Nothing downstream changes, the instant-toggle behaviour you want already works, and Tier 2 needs no new machinery.

---

❓ **Q2** — **One tier at a time, or stacking?**: Is a student in exactly one tier, or can tiers layer (e.g. Tier 3 assigned-only *plus* two features from Tier 2)?

➡️ **Exactly one tier at a time.** Stacking gets confusing fast and you already have a free-form way to express any combination: Tier 2 *is* the arbitrary case. If a Tier 3 student needs drill switched on, that's just moving them to Tier 2 with drill on.

---

❓ **Q3** — **Where does a brand-new signup land?**: Right now every access column defaults to `true`, so a stranger who signs up gets the entire platform. That is the same open-door problem we found this morning, one layer up. When tiers exist, what tier does someone land in the moment they create an account, before you've done anything?

➡️ **Tier 3 (assigned-only).** Nothing is open until you open it, which makes every grant deliberate. They can still sign in, finish the intake, message you and book. The alternative is a "no tier / prospect" state, but that's really just Tier 3 by another name.

---

❓ **Q4** — **When does Tier 1's full access end?**: You said one booked session buys full access, then started to say "10 days" and pulled back. So: does Tier 1 access ever expire on its own? Options: never expires and you downgrade by hand; expires a fixed number of days after the session; or lasts while they have unused session credit and drops when credits hit zero.

➡️ **Never auto-expires; you downgrade by hand.** Automatic revocation is the dangerous one: it fires silently, and the failure mode is a paying student losing their work mid-drill with no warning and no explanation. A stale grant costs you nothing by comparison. If you do want expiry later, it should warn you first and never act on its own.

---

❓ **Q5** — **What a Tier 3 student sees with nothing assigned**: If someone is assigned-only and you haven't assigned anything yet, what's on their screen? Everything locked including the classroom home, or a floor that always stays open?

➡️ **A floor of three: classroom home, messages, schedule.** They need somewhere to land, a way to reach you, and a way to book. That's exactly what `reset_onboarding` already keeps open, so it's an established precedent in the product rather than a new rule. Everything else stays shut until assigned.

# Context Map

The repo has more than one bounded context, each with its own language. This map points to where each glossary lives.

## Contexts

- [Public surface](./src/pages/CONTEXT.md) — the unauthenticated landing + sign-in page (`Auth.tsx`): how LSAT U describes itself to a prospective student.
- [Teacher workspace](./src/pages/teacher/CONTEXT.md) — Joshua's admin operator surface (`/teacher`): assigning prepared material to students and the transcript pipeline.
- [Teacher Desk page](./src/pages/teacher/desk/CONTEXT.md) — the named visual regions of the desk page itself (the boxes on screen), nested under the teacher workspace. Names the layout so we can revise it precisely.
- [Guided Drills & Explanations](./src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md) — the practice domain: guided drills, the question bank, drill instruction cards, and per-question explanations (the Phase 2 authoring work).
- [Classroom](./src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md) — the student's own room: the Classroom frame, its sections, and the flag-versus-wrong-answer distinction.
- [Reading Comprehension](./src/features/rc/CONTEXT.md) — full-passage RC drilling: passages, sessions, attempts, reveal modes, status, and the adaptive picker.
- [Activity Centre](./src/features/activity-centre/CONTEXT.md) — coach-led skill exercises a student is assigned, and the Read, Recall, Colour drill that runs inside one: Chunk, Detail, Coverage, The Recall, Report Card.
- [Booking](./src/components/booking/CONTEXT.md) — paying for and scheduling tutoring time: Packages, Booking Passes, the public microsite, and the Single Session (Slots, Holds, Bookers).

## Relationships

- Both surfaces describe the same product but to different audiences (prospective student vs. operator), so a word can carry different weight in each. Check the right glossary before reusing a term.
- A **flag** (Classroom) and a **wrong answer** (Wrong Answer Journal) are different things on different tables: a flag is chosen mid-question, a wrong answer is earned by missing one. Never collapse the two, and never let "flagged" drift into meaning "struggled with".
- **Set** now means one thing everywhere: the group a student assembles and drills. It used to mean a passage's position within its section in Reading Comprehension; that sense is now called **Position** (the data still carries `setNumber`). The collision was retired when the LR and RC pickers merged into one surface — a primary noun that changed meaning when you flipped the mode was the confusion the merge existed to remove. See `docs/adr/0006-rc-selection-is-question-grain.md`.
