# Teacher Workspace — Context

The teacher workspace (`/teacher`, admin-only) is Joshua's operator surface for running 1:1 LSAT bootcamps. This file is the glossary: what each term means here, so the nav and code stop colliding. It is not a spec.

## Organizing principle

The nav is **verb-first**: organized by what Joshua does, not by artifact type. The central verb is **Assign** — pushing prepared material to a student. The long-term goal is to make assignment fast to fire and eventually automated (driven by session transcripts), pulling from a library of prepared materials.

## Glossary

### Assignable (material type)
A kind of prepared material Joshua can give a student. These stay SEPARATE object types — they are NOT collapsed into one generic item. The types: **Bootcamp**, **Guided Drill**, **Question Set**, **Note**, **Homework**, and (future) **Whiteboard Template**. Each keeps its own builder/editor. What unifies them is not their shape but two shared surfaces: the **Library** (where all of them live) and **Assign** (how any of them reach a student).

### Library (content store)
The single place that holds ALL of Joshua's prepared materials, grouped by type: every Bootcamp, every Guided Drill, every Question Set, every Note, every Whiteboard Template. This is a CONTENT store, not an action menu. (The current app misuses "Library" as a wrapper for assign-actions; that is the bug being fixed.) From the Library Joshua browses/edits material and can assign it.

### Assign (the verb / action)
The action of pushing a Library item to one student with a date. In the redesigned Teacher Workspace, Assign is a top-level area because assigning work is one of Joshua's primary daily jobs. It still has **two doors**, co-equal: (1) from the **Library** — "I have this drill, send it to Lydia"; (2) from a **Student** — "here's Lydia, add a conditional note + the Causation bootcamp." Both doors land in the student's plan/feed. Future: pre-filled / automated from session transcripts.

## Top-level nav (target)

Verb/surface-first, co-equal Library + Students:
- **Today** — the start page for what needs attention now.
- **Students** — door 1: roster -> per-student plan, assign from here, recap, smartboard, conversation, that student's Insights.
- **Assign** — the fast path for giving work to students.
- **Teach** — live teaching tools, teaching materials, review surfaces, and the AI Teaching Assistant.
- **Admin** — accounts + granular feature flags.

Homes still TBD under these areas for: Schedule, Whiteboard, Library, Insights, Conversations, Recaps, Templates, Bootcamp References, Tutor Corpus.

### Bootcamp
A multi-day topic program (Causation Station, Intro to LR, etc.). It is ONE kind of Assignable. NOTE the old collision: two surfaces were both called "Bootcamps" —
- the **assign** action (`TeacherBootcamps`, toggle a bootcamp on for a student) → this is just Assign.
- the **analytics** view (`BootcampsHub`, per-topic completion/accuracy) → this is **Insights**, NOT Assign.
"Bootcamp" the word now means only the program itself. The two jobs are named by their verb.

### Insights
Read-only analytics: per-student and per-topic progress, completion, drill accuracy, flashcard reviews. Absorbs the old top-level "Bootcamps" (topic analytics) and the "Analytics" Library tab.

### Bootcamp Reference
Inspiration material **for Joshua only** (`data/bootcampReferences.ts`): example bootcamps and sources he draws from when *building* his own bootcamps. **Never assigned to students.** This is the hard line that defines the Library: References are teaching-prep for Joshua, so they do NOT belong in the Library (which holds only assignable, student-facing material). References get their own separate top-level home (a personal reference shelf). Only **Bootcamps** — the finished programs — are assignable.

### Library (rule)
**Library holds only things a student can receive.** If Joshua can't assign it to a student, it is not Library material (that rules out Bootcamp References, which are his private inspiration shelf).

========CLASSROOM========
# Classroom

The student's own room: the first surface a 1:1 student lands on. It is a reading surface, not a workspace. Its language is second person, and the coach is never named in the furniture.

## Terms

**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.

**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.

**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.

**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
_Avoid_: Drill page (when the question experience could be confused with the landing surface), Mainhub

**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question opened. It takes free text, matched against question text, and it takes a question address (PT 90, Section 1, Question 12) typed or picked from PrepTest / Section / Question selectors. It searches both banks and groups its results by bank, Logical Reasoning first, because a hit lands differently in each: an LR hit opens that one question, an RC hit opens its whole passage with every question on it. The finder is a finding aid, not the scope of the work it opens. It appears on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing workspace; it never builds a set, it hands that to the Drill console.
_Avoid_: search bar, command palette, spotlight, quick access, find bar

**Drill console** — the control surface inside the Drill landing workspace where a student assembles a set: the filters, the preview of the exact set, and the start action. Finding one named question is not its job; that is the Question finder.
_Avoid_: Control pane, picker card

**Drill canvas** — the route-specific visual composition to the right of the persistent Classroom rail, containing the Drill hero, Drill console, practice/insight workspace, and Today focus. The Classroom rail is not part of the canvas.
_Avoid_: Drill side pane, dashboard grid

**Section** — a middle-column list paired with the reading column: Notes and recaps, Assigned work, Flagged. A section is picked in the rail, lists its items in the middle column, and shows the chosen item in the reading column.

**Flag** — a come-back-to-this marker the student raises *during* a question, from a drill or a blind review. It records intent to return, and says nothing about whether the question was answered correctly. Stored in `flagged_questions`.

**Flagged question** — a question carrying a flag. The Flagged section is the student's unfinished business: questions they chose to set aside. Clearing a flag is the student's own act (Unflag), not something the system does when they get the question right.

**Wrong answer** — a question the student actually missed, recorded in `wrong_answer_journal` and worked through in Wrong answers at `/journal`. Distinct from a flag in both meaning and storage: a flag is chosen, a wrong answer is earned. A question can be both, one, or neither. Do not treat the two lists as views of the same thing.

**Journal entry** — the review of one wrong answer at `/journal/:qid`. The URL is the selection: it resolves only by `qid`, never by the journal row id, and `/journal` does not silently select the first entry. Its review fields autosave without a visible save state.

**Ask** — sending a question to the coach from the surface the student is looking at, with their own comment attached. Always labelled "Ask", never with the coach's name; the name belongs in messaging itself, not in the furniture that leads to it.

## Boundaries

The Classroom owns presentation and the student's own annotations of their work. It does not own the work: assignments, drills, attempts and the question bank all belong to their own contexts and are read here, not authored here.

## assistant

## Where we are

We design three access tiers for students. I asked you five questions. You did not answer them. This is the same set of questions in simple words.

## The background

LSAT U controls student access with 13 switches. Every student record has these switches. One switch opens the Drill. One switch opens the Classroom. Other switches open the other features.

All 13 switches are ON for a new student today. This is a problem. A new person gets the full platform at the first sign-in.

A **tier** is a name for one set of switch positions. You want three tiers:

- **Tier 1** — the student books one Single Session. The student then gets all features.
- **Tier 2** — you select each feature. You can change a feature at any time.
- **Tier 3** — the student opens only the material that you **Assign**.

Two searches of the code are not complete. They will tell us how the system knows about a new Booking Pass. They will also tell us how **Assigned work** gets to a student. You do not need this data to answer the five questions.

## The five questions

**Q1. Does a tier set the 13 switches, or does a tier replace them?**

All parts of the app read the 13 switches now. A replacement is a large change. A replacement also cannot show Tier 2, because Tier 2 is a free selection of features.

➡️ Keep the 13 switches. The tier sets them. Add one new field for the tier name.

**Q2. Can a student have more than one tier at the same time?**

➡️ No. Give one tier to one student. Tier 2 can already show any set of features.

**Q3. Which tier do you give to a new student at the first sign-in?**

➡️ Give Tier 3. Then nothing is open until you open it. The student can still sign in, complete the intake, send you a message, and book a session.

**Q4. Does Tier 1 access stop automatically after some days?**

➡️ No. You remove the access manually. An automatic stop is dangerous. It can close the platform for a student in the middle of a Drill. The student gets no warning.

**Q5. What can a Tier 3 student open when you assign no material?**

➡️ Three items stay open: the Classroom home page, the messages, and the schedule. The student needs a place to land, a way to contact you, and a way to book a session. Everything else stays closed.

Answer these five. Then I continue with the next set.

<result>Findings from the worktree `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu-wt-revoke` (the main repo was not read).

## 1. Tables that record a booking

Two separate booking systems exist side by side, plus a Cal.com mirror and a manual session log.

**`booking_passes`** — `supabase/migrations/20260708111013_ea3424fe-8663-4eaf-b57b-d8dcf55a0000.sql:3`
A row = a credit bucket ("N sessions") issued to one student, addressed by a hashed link token. Key cols: `total_credits`, `used_credits`, `session_length_minutes`, `expires_at`, `student_id`.
Status: `active | exhausted | expired | revoked`, plus `paused` added in `20260719120000_booking_pass_paused_status.sql`. Not a booking — an entitlement.

**`booking_pass_redemptions`** — same file, line 36
A row = one actual scheduled session bought with a pass credit. Cols: `start_at`, `end_at`, `schedule_event_id`, `google_event_id`, `idempotency_key`, later `reschedule_count`, `calendar_status`, `submission_status` (`none|staged|linked|failed`, `20260720180000_booking_phase4_upload_idempotency.sql:8`).
Status: originally `booked | cancelled | failed`; `no_show` added at `20260719120000_booking_pass_paused_status.sql:36-39`.

**`single_session_bookings`** — `supabase/migrations/20260816010000_single_session_schema.sql:6`
A row = one native (non-Cal.com) 70-minute paid session with its whole Stripe lifecycle: hold → checkout → paid → calendar. Carries `stripe_checkout_session_id`, `stripe_payment_intent_id`, `amount_cents`, `gcal_event_id`, `meet_url`, `booking_token_hash`, `cancel_token_hash`, `lifecycle_version`, sweeper lease cols.
Status (`single_session_status_chk`, line ~50): `draft, checkout_open, booked, cancelled, expired, abandoned, refund_pending, refunded, refund_failed`. **`booked` is the only "this is real" state; there is no `completed`.**

**`booking_commerce_events`** — `supabase/migrations/20260810000000_booking_commerce_hub.sql:33`
An append-only provider-event ledger, unique on `(provider, provider_event_id, event_type)` — the idempotency boundary. `provider ∈ stripe|calcom|lsatu`; `event_type` is free text: `'paid'`, `'unmatched_paid'`, Cal trigger names (`BOOKING_CREATED`/`BOOKING_CANCELLED`), single-session finalize events. Not a schedule; an audit log.

**`external_booking_events`** — same file, line 48
One row per Cal.com booking (`provider_booking_id` UNIQUE, upserted), with `start_at`/`end_at`, matched `student_id`, `offer_id`, and a mirror to `google_event_id`/`schedule_event_id`. Status: text defaulting `'booked'`; the webhook only ever writes `'booked'` or `'cancelled'`.

**`schedule_events`** — `supabase/migrations/20260513111010_e4e073dd-2168-4ebe-84cc-0d2cac5ea64c.sql:3`
Google Calendar mirror for the shared admin calendar. `status` default `'confirmed'` (Google's value), `deleted_at` soft-delete. Not student-scoped — do not use it to answer "did this student book".

**`student_sessions`** — `supabase/migrations/20260424020000_create_student_sessions.sql:7`
Joshua's manual log that a tutoring session *happened*: `scheduled_at`, `duration_minutes`, `notes`, `transcript_id`. Status `scheduled | completed | cancelled | no_show`, **default `'completed'`**. Written by admins/session-note ingestion (`supabase/functions/_shared/sessionIngestCore.ts:213`), never by a webhook. This is the only table with a genuine "attended" semantic.

## 2. Webhook handlers

One function, both providers: `supabase/functions/booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts`, dispatched on `?provider=stripe|calcom` (`:349-401`).

Stripe branch:
- `refund.*` → RPC `record_single_session_refund_status` (`:200-212`).
- `checkout.session.expired` → RPC `expire_single_session_checkout` (`:214-227`).
- `checkout.session.completed` with `payment_status === 'paid'` → calls `grantStudentAccess(...)` (`:234-246`), then either:
  - `metadata.product === 'single_session'` → validates amount/currency against `booking_event_types`/`booking_price_cents`, mints a cancel token, calls `finalize_single_session_payment` (`:286-300`) which flips the row to `status='booked'` and inserts a `booking_commerce_events` row (`supabase/migrations/20260915191000_fix_single_session_commerce_offer_reference.sql:94-124`).
  - otherwise matches the payment link to a `booking_offers` row and calls `apply_booking_package_payment` (`:339`), which adds `offer.credits` to the student's newest `booking_passes` row (or creates one), sets it `active`, and logs `event_type='paid'` (`20260810000000_booking_commerce_hub.sql:80-117`). Unmatched payers get `event_type='unmatched_paid'` (`:326-335`).

Cal.com branch (`:353-400`): verifies `x-cal-signature-256`, resolves the offer by event-type slug, fuzzy-matches the student by email/name (`matchStudent`, `:60-80`), mirrors to Google Calendar when matched and booked, then upserts `external_booking_events` (status `booked` or `cancelled` from `triggerEvent === 'BOOKING_CANCELLED'`) and `booking_commerce_events`. **Cal.com bookings never touch `booking_passes` credits.**

There is no "completed" webhook from either provider.

## 3. "Has this student booked at least one session?"

The canonical read path is the edge function `supabase/functions/student-booking/index.ts` action `info` (`:110-160`), which unions:
- RPC `get_student_booking(auth_uid)` → `redemptions` (`supabase/migrations/20260720190000_booking_phase5_student_state.sql:41-53`, joining `booking_pass_redemptions` → `booking_passes` on `student_id`), and
- `single_session_bookings` filtered `.eq('user_id', userId)`,
tagging each `source: 'redemption' | 'single_session'`.

Concrete condition — a student has booked iff either holds:
```
EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM booking_pass_redemptions r
        JOIN booking_passes p ON p.id = r.booking_pass_id
        WHERE p.student_id = $uid AND r.status = 'booked')
OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM single_session_bookings
        WHERE user_id = $uid AND status = 'booked')
```
Add `external_booking_events WHERE student_id = $uid AND status='booked'` only if you want Cal.com bookings, which the client already reads separately as `external_bookings`. The client-side equivalent filter is `src/hooks/useBookingScheduleItems.ts:15` (`booking.status === 'booked'`).

Caveat: `single_session_bookings.user_id` is only set when the buyer was signed in; guest purchases match by email only, and Cal.com matching is a best-effort email/name lookup that can leave `student_id` NULL.

## 4. "Session has happened / credits used up"

There is **no attendance signal from any automated path**. Three distinct notions:

- **Time passing** — the only automatic "used" signal. `src/hooks/useBookingScheduleItems.ts:17` computes `completed = new Date(end_at ?? start_at) &lt; now` purely client-side for display; nothing is persisted.
- **Credits consumed** — `booking_passes.used_credits` increments at *redemption* (booking time), not at attendance: `redeem_booking_pass` does `used_credits = used_credits + 1, status = CASE WHEN used_credits+1 &gt;= total_credits THEN 'exhausted' ELSE 'active' END` (latest version `supabase/migrations/20260718234415_...:61-62`). Cancellation refunds the credit (`20260720160000_booking_phase2_atomic_admin.sql:105`). `get_student_booking` also lazily flips `active → exhausted`/`expired` on read (`20260720190000...:13-21`). So "credits used up" = `booking_passes.status = 'exhausted'` OR `used_credits &gt;= total_credits` — it means *fully booked*, not *fully attended*.
- **Attended** — only manual: `booking_pass_redemptions.status = 'no_show'` set by admin action `mark_no_show` (`supabase/functions/booking-pass-admin/index.ts:445-450`), and `student_sessions.status = 'completed'` written by hand or by session-note ingestion (`supabase/functions/_shared/sessionIngestCore.ts:166-213`). `single_session_bookings` has no completed/attended state at all.

## 5. Automation writing profiles access flags

**Yes, one exists — on payment only, never on booking.**
`supabase/functions/_shared/grantStudentAccess.ts:26-48`: looks up the auth user by the Stripe customer email, then `profiles.update({...all PAID_STUDENT_FLAGS: true}).eq('class_id', user.id)`. Flags listed at `:9-19` (`has_classroom_access, has_practice_access, has_drill_access, has_bootcamp_access, has_journal_access, has_waj_access, has_chat_access, has_schedule_access, has_activity_access`).

Called from exactly one place: `booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts:238`, inside `checkout.session.completed` when `payment_status === 'paid'`, wrapped in try/catch so a failure never fails the webhook. It is fire-and-forget: if the payer has no account yet it returns `{granted:false, reason:'no_account'}` and nothing retries later.

Plainly: there is **no** automation that writes profiles flags in response to a *booking* (Cal.com booking, pass redemption, single-session hold, or session completion), and **nothing ever revokes** flags automatically — by design, per the comment at `grantStudentAccess.ts:5-7`. Revocation is manual via `admin-manage-users`.</result>

## assistant

Both lookups are saved. Here is what they found, in plain words.

## Good news: Tier 1 is half built

The platform already turns on 9 of the 13 switches automatically. It does this when Stripe reports a payment. The code is `grantStudentAccess.ts`.

It does not turn on 4 switches: Analytics, Flagged, Export, and TA.

But note the trigger. It fires on **payment**, not on a booking. A Cal.com booking changes no switches.

## A problem with Tier 1 expiry

I recommended that Tier 1 access must never stop by itself. The code now proves this is the only safe choice.

No system knows that a session happened. Stripe does not send a "session complete" event. Cal.com does not send one either. The Single Session record has no "complete" state at all.

Only you know. You mark it by hand, or the session notes do it.

So a rule like "access stops after the session is used" cannot be built correctly. A time rule or a manual rule are the only real options.

## Good news: Tier 3 has a working model

The Bootcamps already work the way you describe Tier 3. `BootcampGate` looks for an assigned item. It opens the Bootcamp if one exists. It shows "Not assigned yet" if none exists. There is **no switch involved**.

So Tier 3 is not new. It is the Bootcamp rule applied more widely.

## A problem with Tier 3

You gave a practice test section as your example. There are two different things here.

**A timed section can be assigned.** You build a Question Set from one PreTest section and assign it. This works today.

**But the student needs two switches to sit it.** The directions screen needs the Classroom switch. The GO button starts the Drill, which needs the Drill switch.

So a Tier 3 student with the Drill switch OFF can open the directions screen and then press a dead GO button. Assignment-only access cannot work while the runners still check switches.

**A full practice test cannot be assigned at all.** The Testing Centre has no assignment. A student starts a test alone. The Drill switch is the only control. This is the largest gap for Tier 3.

Two notes first. Your screenshot confirms the archive work is live in the Lovable preview. And the reason you cannot "see all the options" is that the per-feature toggle screen exists in the code but is not connected to any page, so there is no way to reach it.

❓ **Q6** — **The one question you did not answer**: Should a tier *set* the 13 switches, or *replace* them?

Everything in the app reads those switches today. If a tier replaces them, every gate in the product has to change. A tier that sets them changes nothing downstream, and Tier 2 is then simply "the switches you picked."

➡️ **The tier sets the switches.** Add one field that records which tier a student is on. The switches stay as the thing the app actually checks. This also makes your instant-toggle requirement free, because flag changes already reach an open tab immediately.

---

❓ **Q7** — **Your two answers disagree about Messages**: On Q3 you said a new student gets "just the homepage, and to be able to schedule" — two things. On Q5 you approved my floor, which was three things: homepage, **messages**, and schedule. I will not pick for you.

➡️ **Include Messages.** When a student hits a locked door, the screen tells them to message you. With Messages off that is a dead end, and a new enrollee has no way to ask why they cannot get in. Schedule alone lets them book but not speak to you.

---

❓ **Q8** — **What did you click?**: You said "when I clicked tier two, it should have given me tier two." Nothing in the code I merged today has a tier control. Did you ask the Lovable agent to build tiers separately, or were you describing what you expect to happen?

➡️ No recommendation — I need to know this before I write anything, or we risk two different tier systems being built at once.

---

❓ **Q9** — **What actually triggers Tier 1**: You said "booked one session." The code triggers on **payment**, not booking, and that path already exists and works. For your Single Session flow these are effectively the same moment, because payment is what marks it booked. A Cal.com booking would not trigger it.

Also: the existing code turns on 9 switches, not 13. It leaves off Analytics, Flagged, Export and TA.

➡️ **Keep payment as the trigger, and raise it to all 13 switches.** Money is the honest signal, and it is already wired. Tier 1 should mean everything, with no quiet exceptions. Cal.com-only bookings stay a manual case.

---

❓ **Q10** — **The Tier 3 problem that has no clean answer yet**: A Tier 3 student has Drill off. You assign a timed section. They open the directions screen, press GO, and hit a dead end, because the runner checks the Drill switch. Assignment-only access cannot work while the runners check switches.

Two ways out. (a) Follow the Bootcamp pattern: an assigned item authorises its own runner, so assigned work opens regardless of switches. (b) Turn on whichever switches the assigned work needs, which quietly gives them the whole Drill.

➡️ **(a), the Bootcamp pattern.** It already works this way for Bootcamps, so it is proven in your product rather than new. It is also the only version that means what you said: strictly what you assign, and nothing else. Option (b) hands a Tier 3 student the entire question bank the first time you assign one section.

---

❓ **Q11** — **Your existing 41 people on the day this ships**: Everyone currently has switches set. What tier do they land on?

➡️ **Map them by what they already have, so nobody's access changes on the day.** The 19 with everything become Tier 1. The 6 partial ones become Tier 2 with their current toggles kept. The 14 you revoked become Tier 3. Then you adjust deliberately afterwards, rather than discovering the change through a student complaint.

## The 13 switches

Grouped as the code groups them, with the label each already has:

**Practice** — Drill · Practice tests · Activities · Bootcamps
**Review** — Wrong answers · Wrong answer journal · Flagged · Analytics and History · Export
**Classroom** — Classroom · Messages · Schedule · TA assignments

Three of those are not what they look like, and it matters because Tier 2 means toggling them one by one:

- **"Practice tests"** does not control the Testing Centre. Sitting a practice test is gated by **Drill**. The Practice tests switch actually controls saved Question Sets.
- **"TA assignments"** does not control the TA route. Opening an assigned TA worksheet is gated by **Classroom**.
- **"Wrong answers"** and **"Wrong answer journal"** are two switches for one page. The route opens if *either* is on.

So Tier 2 today would give you four toggles that lie about what they do. That needs fixing as part of this work, not after.

Saved. Q6, Q7, Q8, Q11 are settled. Round 3.

❓ **Q12** — **Re-asking the one you skipped, because it decides whether Tier 3 works at all**: A Tier 3 student has Drill off. You assign them a timed section. They open the directions screen, press GO, and nothing happens, because the runner checks the Drill switch.

Either (a) an assigned item authorises its own runner, the way Bootcamps already work, so assigned work opens no matter what the switches say; or (b) assigning something turns on the switches it needs, which hands them the whole Drill and the full question bank.

➡️ **(a).** It already works this way for Bootcamps, so it is proven in your product. It is also the only one that means what you said: strictly what you assign. Option (b) makes Tier 3 collapse into Tier 1 the first time you assign anything.

---

❓ **Q13** — **A live automation now contradicts you**: You said Tier 1 happens only when you toggle it. But there is already code that fires on every Stripe payment and switches on 9 of the 13 by itself. Right now, paying grants access without you.

If I simply delete it, a student who pays at 11pm on a Saturday sits locked out until you notice. If I leave it, your "only when I toggle it" rule is false.

➡️ **Replace the automatic grant with a notification.** Payment stops changing access and instead puts "X paid, set their tier" in front of you on Today. You keep control, and nobody is stranded over a weekend because a rule fired silently or failed to.

---

❓ **Q14** — **When you change one switch on a Tier 1 student**: Someone is Tier 1, everything on. You turn Drill off. What tier are they now?

➡️ **They become Tier 2 automatically.** A tier is a template; the moment you deviate, the template no longer describes them. Showing "Tier 1" next to a student who has lost Drill is the same lie as the "Drill on" summary that hid nine closed doors this morning.

---

❓ **Q15** — **Does Tier 2 remember?**: You put someone on Tier 2 with a custom set of toggles. Later you move them to Tier 1, then back to Tier 2. Do their old toggles come back, or do they start from whatever they have now?

➡️ **No memory.** Coming back from Tier 1 means all 13 are on and you turn off what you want. Remembered state that reappears without being asked for is how you end up surprised by someone's access months later.

---

❓ **Q16** — **The four lying switches**: Tier 2 puts all 13 in front of you, so they have to mean what they say. Do I rewire them to match their labels, or rename them to match their wiring?

Rewiring means: "Practice tests" actually gates the Testing Centre, "TA assignments" actually gates TA worksheets, and the two Wrong answer switches merge into one, leaving 12.

➡️ **Rewire, and keep everyone's effective access identical on the day.** The names are right and the wiring is wrong; renaming would bless a mistake. But rewiring does change which switch controls what, so I would set each existing student's switches so that what they can actually reach does not change — which is the promise you already made in Q11.

➡️ **Archive.** It is reversible, it takes them off your list, and their access is already off by default so nothing else needs to happen. Saved. That principle is now the spine of Tier 3: **per item, never per feature.** Assigning one practice test must not hand over the Testing Centre.

One last thing it opens up, then the three you skipped.

❓ **Q28** — **What counts as one practice test?**: A PrepTest in your product is four sections. When you assign "a practice test," do you mean the whole sitting, or the individual sections?

This matters because they're built differently. A timed **section** is already assignable today — it goes out as a Question Set of qids from one section, and the student gets a directions screen and a 35-minute clock. A full **sitting** runs through the Testing Centre, with legs, pacing and a break, and has no assignment path at all.

➡️ **Both, but sections first.** Sections already work, so they come free with the runner change. The full sitting is the genuinely new build. If you mostly assign sections, we ship sections with the tiers and add full sittings right after, rather than holding the whole tier system behind the bigger piece.

---

And the three from the backstop you haven't answered:

❓ **Q29** — **Do students see their tier?** ➡️ **No.** "Tier 3" is operator language. A student should see what they can open, not a rank.

❓ **Q30** — **Do you want a history of tier changes?** ➡️ **Yes, a simple one.** Who changed which student's tier, when, and from what. Cheap to add now, impossible to reconstruct later, and it answers "why does she have Drill?" six weeks on.

❓ **Q31** — **Do tiers touch anything beyond feature access?** Booking rates, session length, pass pricing? ➡️ **No.** Keep tiers as access only. Money already lives in Booking Passes and Single Session with their own rules; tying the two together means a pricing change silently alters someone's access.

Answer these and the tree is bare — I'll write it up as a plan for you to approve before anything gets built.

## What we agreed

**A tier is a template.** It sets the switches; the switches stay what the app actually checks. One tier per student.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Tier 1** | Everything on. You toggle it yourself. Never expires. |
| **Tier 2** | You pick each feature. Instant. No memory. |
| **Tier 3** | Only what you assign, plus the floor. |
| **(no tier)** | Undecided. Same access as Tier 3, but sitting in your queue. |

The floor is Classroom home, Messages, Schedule. Nothing else.

**Tier 1 is all-on by definition**, so turning one switch off relabels them Tier 2. Only the label moves, never the access.

**The spine of Tier 3: per item, never per feature.** Assigning one practice test does not hand over the Testing Centre. Assigning one drill does not hand over the question bank. Assigned work authorises its own runner, the way Bootcamps already do.

**Your rule, stated twice: access never changes by itself.** So the payment automation that currently grants 9 switches behind your back gets removed and becomes a notification.

**The signup queue** sits on both Today and Students, holds new signups and anyone with no tier. Checkmark assigns a tier; X puts them on Tier 3, which is functionally no access and clears them from the queue.

**12 switches, not 13** — the two Wrong answer switches merge. Four were lying about what they control and get rewired, with everyone's effective access held identical through the change.

**Migration day:** 19 → Tier 1, 6 → Tier 2 with their toggles kept, 14 → Tier 3. Those 14 regain Messages and Schedule. That's the one deliberate widening, and it also closes the loose end from this morning, so don't handle them separately.

**Not doing:** students never see tier names, no change history, no link to pricing or session length.

## Two things worth flagging before you approve

**This is a big build.** Roughly: a new tier column and template logic; rewiring four switches plus a data migration that preserves access; per-item authorisation in four separate runners; a brand-new assignment path for full practice tests, which has no table today; the signup queue on two screens; and removing the payment grant. That's several sessions, not one.

**The full practice test is the expensive piece.** Everything else is rewiring things that exist. Per-test assignment is genuinely new. If you want tiers working sooner, that's the natural thing to split into a second stage — but you told me not to defer it, so I've kept it in scope unless you say otherwise.

Say the word and I'll turn this into a written implementation plan with a build order. I haven't written any code, and won't until you confirm this reads right.

Both fixed. The precise version:

**Tier 2 saves your toggles.** They're the student's switch state — of course they persist. If you turn Drill and Activities on for Venusa, that's what she has until you change it.

**What isn't kept is a snapshot.** If you move her Tier 2 → Tier 1 → Tier 2, she doesn't get her old Drill-and-Activities set back. Tier 1 turned everything on, and that's now the truth. You'd turn off what you don't want.

That's the only case my "no memory" phrase was meant to cover, and it was a bad way to say it.

Tier 3 confirmed as written: only what you assign, plus the floor of Classroom home, Messages and Schedule.

# Analytics, History, and Flags — rebuild plan

Date: 2026-08-26
Status: DRAFT, awaiting Joshua's sign-off
Owner: Jay 2
Scope: `/analytics`, `/history`, `/flagged`, the classroom "Notes" rail, plus the logging layer underneath all three.

---

## 1. What Joshua asked for

Verbatim intent, decomposed:

1. Analytics page keeps the question-type breakdown it has now, but becomes a real visual surface — high-end interactive graphs, hover/tap detail, drill-down.
2. Keep the Assumption / Inference family grouping that already exists.
3. Every flagged question must actually reach the flag bank.
4. "Notes and recaps" becomes "Notes".
5. History gets easier filtering, and must contain **all** work — every drill assigned and every drill done, in every form.
6. A 7 / 14 / 21 / 30 / all-time control drives both stats and history.
7. Analytics must cover all three taxonomies: question types, reasoning types, question features.

## 2. Decisions locked in the grill (2026-08-26)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|

What Joshua asked for\n\n1. Three tiers he applies himself, like templates.\n2. **Tier 1** — every feature on, indefinite.\n3. **Tier 2** — he picks each feature, toggled individually, effect is instant.\n4. **Tier 3** — strictly what he assigns, and nothing else.\n5. A signup queue on his admin homepage with a checkmark / X per person.\n6. Switch labels in plain English that say what they actually open.\n\n## 2. Decisions locked in the grill (2026-09-22)\n\n| # | Decision |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | A tier is a **template**. It sets the switches; the switches stay what the app checks. |\n| 2 | **One tier per student.** No stacking. |\n| 3 | **12 switches**, not 13 — the two Wrong answer switches merge. |\n| 4 | Tier 1 = all 12 on, manual only, never expires. |\n| 5 | Tier 1 is all-on **by definition**; turning any switch off relabels the student Tier 2. Label only, never access. |\n| 6 | Tier 2 toggles persist as normal state. No snapshot is restored on leaving and returning. |\n| 7 | Tier 3 = the floor plus assigned items only. |\n| 8 | **The floor** = Classroom home, Messages, Schedule. |\n| 9 | **Per item, never per feature.** Assigning one practice test must not open the Testing Centre. |\n| 10 | Assigned work **authorises its own runner**, following the existing `BootcampGate` pattern. |\n| 11 | **Access never changes by itself.** No expiry, no automatic upgrade or downgrade. |\n| 12 | Payment no longer grants access. It raises a notification instead. |\n| 13 | Signup queue lives on **both** Today and Students. |\n| 14 | Queue holds new signups **and** everyone with no tier. |\n| 15 | Checkmark assigns a tier. **X puts them on Tier 3**, which is functionally no access. |\n| 16 | \"No tier\" = undecided. Same access as Tier 3, but sitting in the queue. |\n| 17 | Migration: 19 → Tier 1, 6 → Tier 2 with toggles kept, 14 → Tier 3 **and they regain Messages and Schedule**. |\n| 18 | A \"practice test\" means the **full sitting**. A section is a separate assignable kind. |\n| 19 | Students never see tier names. |\n| 20 | No history of tier changes. |\n| 21 | Tiers control feature access only — nothing to do with pricing, rates or session length. |\n\n## 3. What exists today (verified 2026-09-22)\n\n- 13 boolean columns on `profiles`, **all defaulting to `true`**. A new signup gets the whole platform.\n- `useUserPermissions` reads them; `ProtectedRoute` gates routes; `railCatalog` padlocks rail items. Admins bypass everything.\n- `profiles` is in the `supabase_realtime` publication, so a switch change reaches an open tab immediately. **Joshua's \"instant toggle\" requirement is already satisfied.**\n- `BootcampGate` is a working per-item gate: `/bootcamp/*` routes carry **no `ProtectedRoute` and no flag**, only an assignment check. This is the proven pattern Tier 3 copies.\n- `grantStudentAccess.ts` fires on Stripe `checkout.session.completed` and switches on 9 of 13 flags automatically. This contradicts decision 11 and is removed in Stage 4.\n- Per-feature toggle UI and Trial/Standard/Full templates already exist in `adminUsers.ts` and `AdminDashboard.tsx`, but `AdminDashboard` is **not mounted on any route**.\n- `practice_test_runs` is self-started (`user_id` defaults to `auth.uid()`). There is **no** assignment path for a full practice test.\n\n### The four mislabelled switches\n\n| Label today | What it really gates | Target |\n|---|---|---|\n| Practice tests (`has_practice_access`) | `/sets` (saved Question Sets) | The Testing Centre |\n| TA assignments (`has_ta_access`) | nothing — `/classroom/ta` uses `has_classroom_access` | TA worksheets |\n| Wrong answers (`has_journal_access`) | `/journal`, opens if **either** journal flag is on | `/journal`, alone |\n| Wrong answer journal (`has_waj_access`) | same page | retired |\n\n## 4. Target switch set (12)\n\n| Group | Label | Column | Gates |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Practice | Drill | `has_drill_access` | `/drill`, `/rc*`, `/sets`, guided drill runners |\n| Practice | Practice tests | `has_practice_access` | `/testing`, `/testing/test/:runId` |\n| Practice | Activities | `has_activity_access` | `/activities*` |\n| Practice | Bootcamps | `has_bootcamp_access` | `/bootcamps` index only |\n| Review | Wrong answers | `has_journal_access` | `/journal*` |\n| Review | Flagged questions | `has_flagged_access` | `/flagged*` |\n| Review | Progress and history | `has_analytics_access` | `/analytics`, `/history` |\n| Review | Export | `has_export_access` | export controls |\n| Classroom | Classroom | `has_classroom_access` | classroom contents |\n| Classroom | Messages | `has_chat_access` | `/inbox*` |\n| Classroom | Schedule and booking | `has_schedule_access` | `/schedule`, `/booking` |\n| Classroom | TA worksheets | `has_ta_access` | `/classroom/ta/:assignmentId` |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Build order\n\nStages 1–5 deliver a working tier system. Stage 6 is what makes Tier 3 mean what Joshua said. Stage 7 is the only genuinely new feature. Each stage is independently shippable and independently revertible.\n\n### Stage 1 — Rewire the switches, merge the journal pair\n\n**Code**\n- `/testing` and `/testing/test/:runId`: `has_drill_access` → `has_practice_access`.\n- `/sets`: `has_practice_access` → `has_drill_access`.\n- `/classroom/ta/:assignmentId`: `has_classroom_access` → `has_ta_access`.\n- `/journal`, `/journal/:qid`: drop the `has_waj_access` alternative, read `has_journal_access` only.\n- `adminUsers.ts`: relabel per section 4, drop `has_waj_access` from the groups.\n- `railCatalog.ts`: update the Testing Centre entry to `has_practice_access`.\n\n**Migration — preserve effective access exactly**\n```sql\nUPDATE profiles SET\n  has_practice_access = has_drill_access,              -- Testing Centre was drill-gated\n  has_ta_access       = has_classroom_access,          -- TA route was classroom-gated\n  has_journal_access  = has_journal_access OR has_waj_access;\n```\n`has_waj_access` is left in place but no longer read. Drop it in a later cleanup once the change has held.\n\n**Known exception, and why it is safe.** Two students (Meghan Mullins, priyanka_nambiar@brown.edu) have Practice on and Drill off. Moving `/sets` under Drill means they lose saved Question Sets. A saved set without Drill is already a dead end — opening one launches `/drill`, which they cannot reach — so nothing usable is lost. No other student is affected.\n\n**Verify.** Write a one-off script that computes every student's reachable routes before and after, and diff. The only permitted difference is the `/sets` case above.\n\n### Stage 2 — The tier column and the template engine\n\n**Migration**\n- `profiles.access_tier text` — `NULL | 'tier_1' | 'tier_2' | 'tier_3'`, with a CHECK constraint. NULL means undecided.\n- Add `access_tier` to `guard_profile_admin_fields()` so students cannot set it on their own row.\n- **Drift trigger** (BEFORE UPDATE on `profiles`): if the row is `tier_1` and any of the 12 switches moves to false, set `access_tier = 'tier_2'`. Implemented in the database so it holds no matter which code path writes.\n\n**Edge function** — `admin-manage-users` gains `set_tier`:\n- `tier_1` → all 12 on.\n- `tier_2` → leave switches untouched; record the label only.\n- `tier_3` → floor on (`classroom`, `chat`, `schedule`), the other nine off.\n\n**UI** — tier control on the Students page, in the expanded panel next to the existing access section. Selecting Tier 2 reveals the 12 toggles grouped as in section 4. Existing single-flag `toggle` action drives them; realtime already makes the effect instant.\n\n### Stage 3 — Migrate the 41, change the defaults\n\n**Migration**\n```sql\n-- Tier by current state, computed AFTER Stage 1's rewire\nUPDATE profiles SET access_tier = 'tier_1' WHERE <all 12 on>;\nUPDATE profiles SET access_tier = 'tier_3' WHERE <classroom only>;\nUPDATE profiles SET access_tier = 'tier_2' WHERE access_tier IS NULL AND <not archived>;\n\n-- The one deliberate widening (decision 17)\nUPDATE profiles SET has_chat_access = true, has_schedule_access = true\nWHERE access_tier = 'tier_3';\n\n-- New signups start at the floor, undecided\nALTER TABLE profiles\n  ALTER COLUMN has_classroom_access SET DEFAULT true,\n  ALTER COLUMN has_chat_access      SET DEFAULT true,\n  ALTER COLUMN has_schedule_access  SET DEFAULT true,\n  ALTER COLUMN has_drill_access     SET DEFAULT false,\n  -- ...and false for the remaining six\n  ALTER COLUMN access_tier          SET DEFAULT NULL;\n```\n\nThis is the change that finally closes the open front door: a stranger who signs up gets the floor and nothing else.\n\n### Stage 4 — Payment stops granting access\n\n- Remove the `grantStudentAccess` call from `booking-commerce-webhook/index.ts`.\n- In its place, insert a `notifications` row (`type = 'payment_needs_tier'`) carrying the payer's email and amount.\n- Delete `_shared/grantStudentAccess.ts` once nothing imports it.\n\n**Risk worth stating plainly.** Until Stage 5 ships, a student who pays gets no access until Joshua acts. Stages 4 and 5 should ship together.\n\n### Stage 5 — The signup queue\n\n- Query: `profiles WHERE access_tier IS NULL AND archived_at IS NULL`, newest first, plus unread `type='signup'` notifications.\n- One shared component rendered on **Today** and on **Students**.\n- Per row: name, email, signup date, and two controls — a checkmark opening a Tier 1 / 2 / 3 picker, and an X that applies `tier_3`.\n- Both write through `set_tier`, so the queue empties as decisions are made.\n\n### Stage 6 — Per-item authorisation (this is what makes Tier 3 real)\n\nA shared `<AssignmentGate>` modelled on `BootcampGate`: it checks that the signed-in student owns an assignment row of the right kind for the id in the route, renders the child if so, and otherwise falls through to the ordinary flag check. Admins bypass, as `BootcampGate` already does.\n\nApplied to:\n- `/section/:assignmentId` and `/classroom/:assignmentId` — homework and timed sections.\n- `/classroom/item/:itemId` — the generic feed item.\n- `/classroom/ta/:assignmentId` — TA worksheets.\n- `/activities/:assignmentId` — Activities.\n- `/guided/:assignmentId`, `/guided-drills/:slug` — guided drills.\n\n**The hard part:** `/drill` is a shared runner behind `DrillLaunchGate`. It must accept a run that is authorised by an assignment rather than by `has_drill_access`. `launchDrill({kind:'assignment'})` already carries the assignment identity, so the gate reads that and authorises the single run — without opening `/drill` generally.\n\n**Note on security.** These gates are route-level. The real protection on the data is RLS, which already scopes every assignment table by `student_id`. A Tier 3 student cannot read someone else's work regardless of this stage.\n\n### Stage 7 — Assignable practice tests (new build)\n\nThe only part with no existing foundation.\n\n- New table `practice_test_assignments`: `student_id`, `pt`, `assigned_by`, `assigned_at`, `unlock_at`, `status`, with RLS matching the other per-student assignment tables.\n- A matching `classroom_items` row, `type = 'practice_test'`, so it appears in Assigned work like everything else.\n- `TestingCentre` lists **only assigned PrepTests** for a student without `has_practice_access`, and the whole bank for one with it.\n- `/testing/test/:runId` wrapped in `AssignmentGate`.\n- The `practice_test_runs` INSERT policy must require either `has_practice_access` or a matching assignment row, so the API cannot be used to start an unassigned test.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Risks\n\n| Risk | Mitigation |\n|---|---|\n| Stage 1 silently changes who can reach what | Before/after reachability diff per student; only the documented `/sets` case may differ |\n| Stage 3 changes column defaults — a mistake locks out every new signup | Ship behind Stage 5 so the queue exists to fix it, and test a real signup end to end |\n| Stage 4 strands a paying student | Ship Stages 4 and 5 together, never 4 alone |\n| Drift trigger fires on admin bulk updates and mass-demotes | Trigger checks for a transition to false on a `tier_1` row only; covered by a test |\n| Tier 3 student hits a dead runner | Stage 6 covers all six routes at once; a partial Stage 6 reintroduces the exact bug this work exists to fix |\n\n## 7. Open items, flagged not assumed\n\n1. **Tier 3 drift.** Decision 5 covers Tier 1 only. If a Tier 3 student has a switch turned on beyond the floor, do they become Tier 2? Consistency says yes. **Not confirmed — needs Joshua.**\n2. **Dropping `has_waj_access`.** Kept for now. Needs a follow-up cleanup once Stage 1 has held.\n3. **Stage 7 sequencing.** Joshua declined to defer it. It is still the largest piece; if tiers are wanted sooner, this is the natural split point.\n\n## 8. Out of scope\n\nStudent-visible tier names, tier change history, and any link between tiers and pricing, booking rates or session length — all explicitly declined (decisions 19, 20, 21).\n\n## 9. Prerequisites outside this plan\n\n1. The PR #130 migration is not applied to the live database.\n2. The frontend is not published to lsatprep.study.\n\nBoth block anything here from being visible.\n"}

## Seven stages

1. **Rewire the switches.** Fix the four that lie, merge the journal pair, 13 → 12. Migration preserves everyone's access exactly.
2. **Tier column and templates.** `access_tier` on profiles, the drift rule as a database trigger so it holds no matter what writes, tier control on the Students page.
3. **Migrate the 41, change the defaults.** This is the stage that finally shuts the open front door — new signups get the floor instead of everything.
4. **Payment stops granting access.** The auto-grant comes out, a notification replaces it.
5. **The signup queue** on Today and Students.
6. **Per-item authorisation.** The `AssignmentGate`, across all six routes. This is what makes Tier 3 mean what you said.
7. **Assignable practice tests.** The only piece with no existing foundation.

Each stage ships and reverts on its own.

## Three things in there you should look at specifically

**Stages 4 and 5 must ship together.** If payment stops granting access before the queue exists, someone who pays on a Saturday night sits locked out with nothing on your screen telling you. I've written that as a hard rule rather than a preference.

**One student-facing exception I couldn't avoid in Stage 1.** Moving saved Question Sets under Drill means Meghan and priyanka lose them, since they have Practice on and Drill off. I checked whether that costs them anything real: opening a saved set launches `/drill`, which they can't reach, so it's already a dead end for them. Nothing usable is lost. Flagged in the plan rather than buried.

**One question I genuinely couldn't answer for you.** You settled that a Tier 1 student who loses a switch becomes Tier 2. You didn't cover the mirror case: a Tier 3 student who *gains* a switch beyond the floor. Consistency says they become Tier 2, but you didn't say it, so I've left it in Open Items instead of deciding on your behalf.

## Before any of it is visible

Still true from earlier: the #130 migration isn't applied, and nothing is published to lsatprep.study. Also worth knowing — Lovable pushed a production build fix to `main` while we were talking, so something else is moving in that repo.

Read it over. Tell me which stage to start, or what to change first.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

# Session Handoff — LSAT U student access: fixed a broken revoke, added archive/delete, designed a three-tier access model

## Where it started
Joshua asked how the platform's student access control currently works, wanting a better on/off toggle. Investigation found "Revoke All Access" did not actually revoke all access. That expanded into shipping two fixes, then a full `/grill-me` interview designing a three-tier access system, ending in a written implementation plan awaiting sign-off.

## Decisions locked + what shipped

- **PR #129 — revoke actually revokes** (merged to main, `b047193`). Six gaps closed: `has_classroom_access` was force-kept true; `/guided/:assignmentId` and `/guided-drills/:slug` had no guard at all; `/conditional-indicators-refresher` and `/classroom/uploads` admitted any signed-in user; `has_activity_access` was missing from the DB guard trigger so students could self-grant it; permissions cached 5 min with no invalidation.
- **`profiles` added to `supabase_realtime`** so a flag change reaches an open tab immediately. Migration `20260922120000_profiles_realtime_and_activity_guard.sql`. Applied to the live DB via the Lovable agent.
- **PR #130 — archive + permanent delete** (merged to main, `dbeeac5`). Archive: all flags off, `archived_at` stamped, hidden from list and count, reversible. Delete: removes auth user, requires typed email match, primary admin protected, self-delete blocked. `student_sessions.student_id` changed from NOT NULL/CASCADE to nullable/SET NULL so Joshua's session records survive a delete; `deleted_students` table + BEFORE DELETE trigger snapshots identity and backfills `former_student_id`.
- **Dropped a planned `auth.admin.signOut(class_id)` call** mid-build — that API takes a JWT, not a user id, so it would have silently failed on every archive. UI copy corrected to match.
- **Tier design settled** across 8 interview rounds. A tier is a template that sets the switches; switches remain enforcement. 12 switches, not 13. Tier 1 = all on, manual, indefinite, relabels to Tier 2 on any deviation. Tier 2 = individual toggles, persist as normal state, no snapshot restored. Tier 3 = floor (Classroom/Messages/Schedule) + assigned items only. Governing rule, stated twice by Joshua: **access never changes by itself**. Per item, never per feature.
- **Plan written**, pushed to branch `docs/student-access-tiers-plan` (`0d01fa9`), NOT merged — awaiting sign-off.
